Conventions. Rings are commutative with \(1\). Results of the text are cited by their numbers in Liu (“Prop. 1.12”, “Thm. 2.19”, …), and results of earlier chapters as “Prop. 3.3.6”, “Ex. 2.4.8” etc. Exercise labels are those of the book, numbered within each section of the chapter: for instance Exercise 2.10 is the tenth exercise at the end of Section 5.2. Each exercise is restated before its solution; sub-items that the book displays as nested lists are written as running prose.

Honest notes. Several printed statements are false as printed or need a hypothesis, and each solution says what is proved instead; in particular Ex. 1.8 (false for subsheaves of an arbitrary module), 1.24 (counterexample in characteristic different from \(2,3\)), 1.29(b),(c) (counterexample with a disconnected \(X'\)), 3.5(a),(b) (false when the special fibre is \(0\)-dimensional), and 3.12 (the geometrically integral case is proved only for \(Y\) integral and \(f\) flat). Partly proved: in Ex. 2.17(c) associativity of the cup product is proved but the general anti-commutativity sign rule only for \(p=0\) or \(q=0\), and one statement of Section 5.2 (the commutation with direct limits) is proved only for finite coverings. Each section below begins with its own list of notes. Some solutions cite standard results which are named where used.

5.1 Coherent sheaves on a scheme

Conventions for this section. Results of the text are cited by their numbers in Liu (“Prop. 1.5”, “Thm. 1.7”, …, all in Section 5.1). For a morphism \(f:X\to Y\) of ringed spaces, \(f^\#:\mathcal{O}_Y\to f_*\mathcal{O}_X\) makes \(f_*\mathcal{F}\) an \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-module, and \(\rho:f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y\to\mathcal{O}_X\) denotes the adjoint homomorphism. Recall that \(f^{-1}\mathcal{G}\) is the sheaf associated to the presheaf \(U\mapsto\varinjlim_{V\supseteq f(U)}\mathcal{G}(V)\), that \((f^{-1}\mathcal{G})_x=\mathcal{G}_{f(x)}\), and that \(f^*\mathcal{G}=f^{-1}\mathcal{G}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y}\mathcal{O}_X\). For an affine scheme \(\operatorname{Spec}A\) and an \(A\)-module \(M\), \(\widetilde M\) is the associated sheaf; \(\widetilde M(D(f))=M_f\). A coherent sheaf is quasi-coherent: by Def. 1.10, near any point there is a surjection \(\mathcal{O}^n\to\mathcal{F}\) whose kernel is again finitely generated, so (shrinking the neighborhood) there is a surjection \(\mathcal{O}^m\to\operatorname{Ker}\), i.e. locally an exact sequence \(\mathcal{O}^m\to\mathcal{O}^n\to\mathcal{F}\to0\) (Def. 1.4); this is what allows us to apply Prop. 1.11 and Thm. 1.7 to coherent sheaves. We use freely that on a locally Noetherian scheme every affine open subset has a Noetherian ring, and that the principal open sets form a base of an affine scheme, so that a morphism of sheaves is determined by its values on principal opens, and compatible families of maps on principal opens glue to a morphism of sheaves.

Honest notes. Ex. 1.8 is false as printed (for subsheaves of an arbitrary \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module \(\mathcal{H}\)): a counterexample is given and the statement is proved when \(\mathcal{H}\) is quasi-coherent. In Ex. 1.1(a) the word “immersion” is made precise (homeomorphism onto its image with surjective maps on stalks, as for immersions of schemes). In Ex. 1.10(c) the sheaf is described by its sections over affine opens, as the statement says.

Exercise 1.1

Problem

Let \(f:X\to Y\) be a continuous map of ringed topological spaces. Let \(\mathcal{F}\) be an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module and \(\mathcal{G}\) an \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-module. (a) Show that there exists a canonical homomorphism \(f^*f_*\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}\) (resp. \(\mathcal{G}\to f_*f^*\mathcal{G}\)) of \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-modules (resp. of \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-modules) (use Exercise 2.2.13(a)). Show that the first homomorphism is an isomorphism if \(f\) is an immersion. (b) Show that there exists a canonical isomorphism \(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(f^*\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_Y}(\mathcal{G},f_*\mathcal{F})\). (c) Show that there exists a canonical homomorphism \(\mathcal{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_Y}f_*\mathcal{O}_X\to f_*f^*\mathcal{G}\) and that it is an isomorphism if \(f\) is an affine morphism of schemes and if \(\mathcal{G}\) is quasi-coherent (note that \(f_*\mathcal{O}_X\) is quasi-coherent).

Solution

Two adjunctions. We first prove two facts, which give (b) and which produce the maps of (a). Regard \(\mathcal{F}\) as an \(f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y\)-module through \(\rho\).

(A) For an \(f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y\)-module \(\mathcal{M}\) and an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module \(\mathcal{F}\), restriction along \(m\mapsto m\otimes1\) gives a bijection

\[ \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(\mathcal{M}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y}\mathcal{O}_X,\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\operatorname{Hom}_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{F}). \]

Indeed, given an \(f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y\)-linear \(\lambda:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{F}\), the maps \(\mathcal{M}(U)\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y(U)}\mathcal{O}_X(U)\to\mathcal{F}(U)\), \(m\otimes a\mapsto a\lambda(m)\) are well defined (for \(r\in f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y(U)\) we have \(\lambda(rm)=\rho(r)\lambda(m)\)), \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)\)-linear and compatible with restrictions. By the universal property of the sheaf associated to a presheaf they induce an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-linear map \(\mathcal{M}\otimes\mathcal{O}_X\to\mathcal{F}\) extending \(\lambda\). It is the only one: an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-linear map is determined on the presheaf \(U\mapsto\mathcal{M}(U)\otimes\mathcal{O}_X(U)\) by its values on the elements \(m\otimes1\) (which generate), and a map out of the associated sheaf is determined by its restriction to the presheaf.

(B) For an \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-module \(\mathcal{G}\) and an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module \(\mathcal{F}\) there is a bijection

\[ \operatorname{Hom}_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y}(f^{-1}\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_Y}(\mathcal{G},f_*\mathcal{F}). \]

Let \(\iota:\mathcal{G}(V)\to(f^{-1}\mathcal{G})(f^{-1}V)\) be the canonical map (class of \(s\) in the presheaf, then sheafify). To \(\theta\) associate \(\psi_V=\theta_{f^{-1}V}\circ\iota:\mathcal{G}(V)\to\mathcal{F}(f^{-1}V)\). For \(a\in\mathcal{O}_Y(V)\), \(\iota(as)=\rho(a)\cdot\iota(s)\) (where we write \(\rho(a)\) for the image of \(a\) in \(f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y(f^{-1}V)\) and then in \(\mathcal{O}_X(f^{-1}V)\), which is \(f^\#(a)\)), so \(\psi_V(as)=f^\#(a)\psi_V(s)\) and \(\psi\) is \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-linear. Conversely let \(\psi:\mathcal{G}\to f_*\mathcal{F}\) be \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-linear. On the presheaf \(P(U)=\varinjlim_{V\supseteq f(U)}\mathcal{G}(V)\) define \(P(U)\to\mathcal{F}(U)\) by sending the class of \(s\in\mathcal{G}(V)\) to \(\psi_V(s)|_U\) (this makes sense since \(U\subseteq f^{-1}V\), and it is compatible with the transition maps because \(\psi\) commutes with restrictions). It induces \(\theta:f^{-1}\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{F}\). It is \(f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y\)-linear: for \(b\in\mathcal{O}_Y(V')\), \(s\in\mathcal{G}(V'')\) representing sections over \(U\subseteq f^{-1}(V'\cap V'')\), we get \(\theta(bs)=\psi(bs)|_U=f^\#(b)\psi(s)|_U=\rho(b)\theta(s)\). The two constructions are inverse to each other, since both are checked on the presheaf level where they are obvious.

Composing, \(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(f^*\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_Y}(f^{-1}\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_Y}(\mathcal{G},f_*\mathcal{F})\). This is the canonical isomorphism asked for in (b). Both bijections are visibly additive (indeed natural in \(\mathcal{F}\) and \(\mathcal{G}\)), which gives (b).

(a) Define \(\eta:\mathcal{G}\to f_*f^*\mathcal{G}\) as the \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-linear map corresponding to \(\mathrm{id}_{f^*\mathcal{G}}\) under (b) (with \(\mathcal{F}=f^*\mathcal{G}\)): explicitly \(\eta_V(s)=\iota(s)\otimes1\in(f^*\mathcal{G})(f^{-1}V)\). Define \(\varepsilon:f^*f_*\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}\) as the \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-linear map corresponding under (b) to \(\mathrm{id}_{f_*\mathcal{F}}\): explicitly it sends \(t\otimes a\), with \(t\) the class of a section \(t'\in\mathcal{F}(f^{-1}V)\), to \(a\cdot t'|_U\). These are the canonical homomorphisms.

Now let \(f\) be an immersion, i.e. a homeomorphism onto its image such that \(\mathcal{O}_{Y,f(x)}\to\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\) is surjective for all \(x\) (this holds for immersions of schemes: a closed immersion followed by an open immersion). We check that \(\varepsilon\) is an isomorphism on stalks at \(x\in X\); put \(y=f(x)\). By Prop. 1.14(a), \((f^*f_*\mathcal{F})_x=(f_*\mathcal{F})_y\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}}\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\), and \(\varepsilon_x\) is \(u\otimes a\mapsto a\,\bar u\) where \(\bar u\) is the image of \(u\) under the canonical map \((f_*\mathcal{F})_y=\varinjlim_{V\ni y}\mathcal{F}(f^{-1}V)\to\mathcal{F}_x\). Since \(f\) is a homeomorphism onto its image, every open neighborhood of \(x\) is of the form \(f^{-1}V\) with \(V\ni y\) open, and one can shrink \(V\) at will; hence this canonical map is an isomorphism. It remains to see that \(\mathcal{F}_x\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}}\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\to\mathcal{F}_x\), \(u\otimes a\mapsto au\) is an isomorphism. Write \(R=\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}\) and \(S=\mathcal{O}_{X,x}=R/I\). Then \(\mathcal{F}_x\otimes_RS=\mathcal{F}_x/I\mathcal{F}_x=\mathcal{F}_x\) because \(I\) acts as \(0\) on the \(S\)-module \(\mathcal{F}_x\), and under this identification the map is the identity. So \(\varepsilon\) is an isomorphism.

(c) For \(V\subseteq Y\) open, define

\[ \mathcal{G}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_Y(V)}(f_*\mathcal{O}_X)(V)=\mathcal{G}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_Y(V)}\mathcal{O}_X(f^{-1}V)\longrightarrow(f^*\mathcal{G})(f^{-1}V)=(f_*f^*\mathcal{G})(V),\quad s\otimes a\mapsto\eta_V(s)\cdot a. \]

This is well defined: for \(b\in\mathcal{O}_Y(V)\), \(\eta_V(bs)\,a=\eta_V(s)\,f^\#(b)a\) because \(\eta_V\) is \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-linear and the structure of \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-module on \(f_*f^*\mathcal{G}\) goes through \(f^\#\). It is compatible with restrictions, so it is a morphism from the presheaf \(V\mapsto\mathcal{G}(V)\otimes\mathcal{O}_X(f^{-1}V)\) to the sheaf \(f_*f^*\mathcal{G}\), and it factors uniquely through the associated sheaf \(\mathcal{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_Y}f_*\mathcal{O}_X\). Call the resulting \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-linear map \(\psi\).

Now assume \(f\) affine and \(\mathcal{G}\) quasi-coherent. The statement is local on \(Y\) and the construction of \(\psi\) is compatible with restriction to open subsets of \(Y\) (replace \(X\) by \(f^{-1}V\)), so we may assume \(Y=\operatorname{Spec}A\), hence \(X=\operatorname{Spec}B\) is affine, and \(\mathcal{G}=\widetilde M\) with \(M=\mathcal{G}(Y)\) (Thm. 1.7). The sheaf \(f_*\mathcal{O}_X\) is \(\widetilde B\) (\(B\) viewed as an \(A\)-module) since \((f_*\mathcal{O}_X)(D(a))=B_{a}\) for \(a\in A\) (here \(D(a)\) has preimage \(D(f^\#a)\) and \(B_{f^\#a}=B\otimes_AA_a\)). By Prop. 1.5(d), \(\mathcal{G}\otimes f_*\mathcal{O}_X=(M\otimes_AB)^\sim\), with global sections \(M\otimes_AB\). The target \(f_*f^*\mathcal{G}\) is quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.14(b),(c). So \(\psi\) is a morphism of quasi-coherent sheaves on the affine scheme \(Y\), and it is an isomorphism as soon as \(\psi(Y)\) is: indeed, both sheaves are associated to modules and \(\psi\) is \(\mathcal{O}_Y\)-linear, so for \(a\in A\) and a section \(a^{-n}u|_{D(a)}\) (\(u\) a global section) of the source over \(D(a)\) we get \(\psi_{D(a)}(a^{-n}u|_{D(a)})=a^{-n}\psi(Y)(u)|_{D(a)}\); thus \(\psi\) is the map induced by \(\psi(Y)\) on every \(D(a)\), namely its localization at \(a\), and localization preserves isomorphisms. Now \(\psi(Y):M\otimes_AB\to(f^*\mathcal{G})(X)\) is \(m\otimes b\mapsto\eta_Y(m)\,b\). This is the map \(\Phi_M\) which, by Prop. 1.14(b), is an isomorphism: indeed in the proof of Prop. 1.14(b) one takes a presentation \(K\to L\to M\to0\) with \(K,L\) free, uses that \(f^*\) of the corresponding sequence of sheaves is right exact, and compares with \(K\otimes B\to L\otimes B\to M\otimes B\to0\); for free modules \(A^{(I)}\) the map \(\Phi\) is the identity \(B^{(I)}\to B^{(I)}\) (since \(f^*\mathcal{O}_Y^{(I)}=\mathcal{O}_X^{(I)}\) and \(X\) is quasi-compact, so global sections commute with direct sums), \(\Phi\) is natural in \(M\), and both functors \(M\mapsto M\otimes_AB\) and \(M\mapsto(f^*\widetilde M)(X)\) are right exact (the latter by Prop. 1.8 applied to the quasi-coherent sheaves \(f^*\widetilde K,f^*\widetilde L,f^*\widetilde M\) and the quasi-coherent images/kernels of Prop. 1.12(c)). The five lemma gives that \(\Phi_M\) is an isomorphism. Hence \(\psi\) is an isomorphism. ∎

Exercise 1.2

Problem

Let \(A\) be an integral domain with field of fractions \(K\), and let \(x_0\in X=\operatorname{Spec}A\). We construct a sheaf \(\mathcal{F}\) on \(X\) by setting \(\mathcal{F}(U)=K\) if \(x_0\in U\) and \(\mathcal{F}(U)=0\) otherwise. The restriction maps are the obvious ones. Show that \(\mathcal{F}\) is an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module. Under what condition is \(\mathcal{F}\) quasi-coherent?

Solution

The restriction maps are \(\mathrm{id}_K\) if \(x_0\in V\subseteq U\) and \(0\) otherwise. This is a presheaf. It is a sheaf: let \(U=\bigcup U_i\) and \(s_i\in\mathcal{F}(U_i)\) with \(s_i|_{U_i\cap U_j}=s_j|_{U_i\cap U_j}\). If \(x_0\notin U\) then all \(\mathcal{F}(U_i)=0\) and \(\mathcal{F}(U)=0\), and there is nothing to prove. If \(x_0\in U\), the \(U_i\) containing \(x_0\) have \(s_i\in K\), and for two of them the overlap contains \(x_0\), so \(s_i=s_j=:\lambda\). The element \(\lambda\in\mathcal{F}(U)\) restricts to \(s_i\) for \(x_0\in U_i\), and to \(0=s_i\) for \(x_0\notin U_i\). It is unique, since \(\mathcal{F}(U)=K\to\mathcal{F}(U_i)=K\) is the identity for some \(U_i\ni x_0\).

Module structure: let \(\mathfrak{p}\) be the prime corresponding to \(x_0\). For \(x_0\in U\) let \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)\) act on \(\mathcal{F}(U)=K\) through \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)\to\mathcal{O}_{X,x_0}=A_\mathfrak{p}\subseteq K\) (germ at \(x_0\) followed by the inclusion into \(K\), \(A\) being a domain); for \(x_0\notin U\) the module \(\mathcal{F}(U)=0\) is trivial. Then \(\mathcal{F}(U)\) is an \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)\)-module. For \(V\subseteq U\) and \(a\in\mathcal{O}_X(U)\), \(s\in\mathcal{F}(U)\): if \(x_0\in V\) then \(a|_V\) and \(a\) have the same germ at \(x_0\), so \((as)|_V=a|_Vs|_V\); if \(x_0\notin V\) both sides are \(0\). So \(\mathcal{F}\) is an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module.

Claim: \(\mathcal{F}\) is quasi-coherent if and only if \(x_0\) is the generic point of \(X\), i.e. \(\mathfrak{p}=(0)\).

Suppose \(\mathfrak{p}=0\). Then \(x_0\in U\) iff \(U\neq\emptyset\) (the generic point lies in every nonempty open set). Now \(D(f)\neq\emptyset\) iff \(f\neq0\), and for \(f\neq0\) we have \(K_f=K\) (\(f\) is already invertible in \(K\)), while \(D(0)=\emptyset\). Hence \(\mathcal{F}(D(f))=K=\widetilde K(D(f))\) for \(f\neq0\) and \(\mathcal{F}(D(0))=0\), with identity restriction maps (and the \(A_f\)-module structures agree: \(A_f\subseteq K\) acts by multiplication). Since the principal opens form a base, \(\mathcal{F}\simeq\widetilde K\), which is quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.5(c).

Suppose \(\mathfrak{p}\neq0\) and, for contradiction, that \(\mathcal{F}\) is quasi-coherent. By Thm. 1.7, \(\mathcal{F}\simeq\widetilde M\) with \(M=\mathcal{F}(X)=K\) (as an \(A\)-module, \(A\subseteq K\)). Pick \(0\neq f\in\mathfrak{p}\). Then \(x_0\notin D(f)\), so \(\mathcal{F}(D(f))=0\), but \(\widetilde M(D(f))=K_f=K\neq0\). An isomorphism of sheaves cannot exist; contradiction. (If \(A\) is a field, \(X\) is one point, \(x_0\) is the generic point, and \(\mathcal{F}=\widetilde K\).) ∎

Exercise 1.3

Problem

Let \(X\) be a scheme and \(f\in\mathcal{O}_X(X)\). Show that the sheaf \(f\mathcal{O}_X\) (Exercise 2.3.4) is finitely generated quasi-coherent. Show that in general it is not an invertible sheaf.

Solution

Recall that \(f\mathcal{O}_X\) is the sheaf of ideals with \((f\mathcal{O}_X)(U)=f|_U\mathcal{O}_X(U)\) for \(U\) affine. Let \(\mu:\mathcal{O}_X\to\mathcal{O}_X\) be multiplication by \(f\). We show \(f\mathcal{O}_X=\operatorname{Im}\mu\). Let \(U=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be affine and \(a=f|_U\in A\). By Prop. 1.5(b), applied to the exact sequence \(A\xrightarrow{a}A\to A/aA\to0\) and to \(0\to aA\to A\), the sheaf \(\operatorname{Im}(\mu|_U)\) is \((aA)^\sim\subseteq\widetilde A=\mathcal{O}_U\), whose sections over \(U\) are \(aA=f|_U\mathcal{O}_X(U)\). So \(\operatorname{Im}\mu\) and \(f\mathcal{O}_X\) are subsheaves of \(\mathcal{O}_X\) with the same sections over each affine open, hence equal.

\(\mathcal{O}_X\) is quasi-coherent, so \(\operatorname{Im}\mu\) is quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.12(c). It is finitely generated because \(\mu:\mathcal{O}_X\to f\mathcal{O}_X\) is a surjective homomorphism (Def. 1.10 with \(n=1\), \(U=X\)).

Not invertible in general. Stalks of \(f\mathcal{O}_X\) are \((f\mathcal{O}_X)_x=f_x\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\) (image of the stalk map). If \(f\mathcal{O}_X\) were invertible, then for each \(x\) the module \(f_x\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\) would be isomorphic to \(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\), in particular have zero annihilator in \(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\). Two examples where this fails: (i) \(f=0\) on a nonempty scheme: \(f\mathcal{O}_X=0\), whose stalks are \(0\neq\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\). (ii) \(X=\operatorname{Spec}k[T]/(T^2)\) (\(k\) a field), \(f=T\): the unique stalk is \(T\cdot k[T]/(T^2)\), which is annihilated by \(T\), whereas \(T\neq0\) in \(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}=k[T]/(T^2)\). So \(f\mathcal{O}_X\) is not invertible, although \(X\) is Noetherian and \(f\) is neither a unit nor zero. (If \(X\) is integral and \(f\neq0\), then multiplication by \(f\) is injective on stalks and \(f\mathcal{O}_X\simeq\mathcal{O}_X\), so one needs zero divisors, or \(f=0\), for a counterexample.) ∎

Exercise 1.4

Problem

Let \(X=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be an affine scheme. Let \(\mathcal{F}\) be a quasi-coherent \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module. Show that for any affine open subset \(U\) of \(X\), we have a canonical isomorphism \(\mathcal{F}(X)\otimes_A\mathcal{O}_X(U)\simeq\mathcal{F}(U)\). Show that this is false if \(U\) is not affine (consider Example 2.3.6 with \(M=\mathbf{Z}[T]/(T,p)\)).

Solution

Put \(M=\mathcal{F}(X)\), so that \(\mathcal{F}\simeq\widetilde M\) (Thm. 1.7). Let \(U=\operatorname{Spec}B\) be an affine open subset of \(X\); then \(B=\mathcal{O}_X(U)\) is an \(A\)-algebra. The canonical map is

\[ \theta:M\otimes_AB\to\mathcal{F}(U),\qquad m\otimes b\mapsto m|_U\cdot b. \]

\(\theta\) is an isomorphism. The sets \(D(g)\subseteq U\), \(g\in A\), cover \(U\); since \(U\) is quasi-compact, \(U=D(g_1)\cup\dots\cup D(g_r)\). Each \(D(g_i)\) is a principal open set of \(U\), namely \(D_U(g_i|_U)\), and \(B_{g_i}=\mathcal{O}_X(D(g_i))=A_{g_i}\). The \(g_i\) generate the unit ideal of \(B\) (as \(\operatorname{Spec}B=\bigcup D_U(g_i)\)). A homomorphism \(\theta\) of \(B\)-modules is an isomorphism as soon as every \(\theta_{g_i}\) is: kernel and cokernel have all localizations \(0\) at the \(g_i\), and if \(\sum b_ig_i^{n}=1\) (possible since the \(g_i\) generate the unit ideal, hence so do their powers), every element killed by some power of each \(g_i\) is \(0\). So it suffices that \(\theta_{g_i}\) is an isomorphism. We have \((M\otimes_AB)_{g_i}=M_{g_i}\otimes_{A_{g_i}}B_{g_i}=M_{g_i}\) (using \(B_{g_i}=A_{g_i}\)). Applying Prop. 1.6 to \(\mathcal{F}|_U\) on the affine scheme \(U\) and \(g_i|_U\) gives an isomorphism \(\mathcal{F}(U)_{g_i}\to\mathcal{F}(D(g_i))\), and Prop. 1.6 applied to \(\mathcal{F}\) on \(X\) gives \(M_{g_i}\simeq\mathcal{F}(D(g_i))\). The composite

\[ M_{g_i}=(M\otimes_AB)_{g_i}\xrightarrow{\theta_{g_i}}\mathcal{F}(U)_{g_i}\to\mathcal{F}(D(g_i)) \]

sends \(m/g_i^n\otimes1\) to \(g_i^{-n}m|_{D(g_i)}\), so it is the isomorphism \(M_{g_i}\simeq\mathcal{F}(D(g_i))\). As the second arrow is an isomorphism, so is \(\theta_{g_i}\). Hence \(\theta\) is an isomorphism.

Failure for non-affine \(U\). Let \(A=\mathbf{Z}[T]\), \(p\) a prime number, \(\mathfrak{m}=(T,p)\), a maximal ideal (\(A/\mathfrak{m}=\mathbf{F}_p\)), \(x\) the corresponding closed point, \(M=A/\mathfrak{m}=\mathbf{F}_p\) and \(\mathcal{F}=\widetilde M\). Let \(U=X\setminus\{x\}=D(T)\cup D(p)\). (It is not affine, as the computation below shows; we do not need this.) Since \(\mathfrak{m}M=0\), for every prime \(\mathfrak{q}\neq\mathfrak{m}\) there is an element of \(\mathfrak{m}\setminus\mathfrak{q}\), invertible in \(A_\mathfrak{q}\) and killing \(M_\mathfrak{q}\), so \(M_\mathfrak{q}=\mathcal{F}_\mathfrak{q}=0\). A section of a sheaf all of whose stalks over \(U\) vanish is \(0\), so \(\mathcal{F}(U)=0\). On the other hand, \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)=\{(a,b)\in A_T\times A_p: a=b\text{ in }A_{Tp}\}=A_T\cap A_p\) inside \(\operatorname{Frac}A\) (\(A\) is a domain). Since \(A=\mathbf{Z}[T]\) is a UFD and \(T,p\) are non-associate primes, \(A_T\cap A_p=A\): if \(a/T^n=b/p^m\) with \(a,b\in A\), then \(ap^m=bT^n\), so \(T^n\mid a\) and \(a/T^n\in A\). So \(\mathcal{F}(X)\otimes_A\mathcal{O}_X(U)=\mathbf{F}_p\otimes_AA=\mathbf{F}_p\neq0=\mathcal{F}(U)\), and the canonical map is not an isomorphism. ∎

Exercise 1.5

Problem

Let \(X\) be a scheme, and let \(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\) be \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-modules. (a) Show that \(U\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_X|_U}(\mathcal{F}|_U,\mathcal{G}|_U)\) defines an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module. This sheaf will be denoted \(\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\). (b) Let us suppose \(X=\operatorname{Spec}A\). Show that the canonical map \(\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\to\operatorname{Hom}_A(\mathcal{F}(X),\mathcal{G}(X))\) is bijective if \(\mathcal{F}\) is quasi-coherent.

Solution

(a) Write \(H(U)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_X|_U}(\mathcal{F}|_U,\mathcal{G}|_U)\) (an abelian group). For \(V\subseteq U\) the restriction \(\varphi\mapsto\varphi|_V\) is defined, and \(H\) is a presheaf. \(H(U)\) is an \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)\)-module via \((a\varphi)_W(s)=a|_W\,\varphi_W(s)\) for \(W\subseteq U\) open, \(s\in\mathcal{F}(W)\); one checks that \(a\varphi\) is again \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-linear (since \(\mathcal{O}_X(W)\) is commutative) and the module axioms. Clearly \((a\varphi)|_V=a|_V\varphi|_V\). It remains to check the sheaf property. Let \(U=\bigcup U_i\).

Separation: if \(\varphi|_{U_i}=0\) for all \(i\), then for \(W\subseteq U\) and \(s\in\mathcal{F}(W)\) the section \(\varphi_W(s)\in\mathcal{G}(W)\) restricts to \(\varphi_{W\cap U_i}(s|_{W\cap U_i})=0\) on each \(W\cap U_i\), so \(\varphi_W(s)=0\).

Gluing: given \(\varphi^{(i)}\in H(U_i)\) with \(\varphi^{(i)}|_{U_i\cap U_j}=\varphi^{(j)}|_{U_i\cap U_j}\), define for \(W\subseteq U\) open and \(s\in\mathcal{F}(W)\) the section \(\varphi_W(s)\in\mathcal{G}(W)\) by gluing the sections \(\varphi^{(i)}_{W\cap U_i}(s|_{W\cap U_i})\) of \(\mathcal{G}\) over the cover \(\{W\cap U_i\}\); they agree on the overlaps by hypothesis. The family \(\varphi_W\) is compatible with restrictions and is \(\mathcal{O}_X(W)\)-linear (check after restriction to each \(W\cap U_i\)), and restricts to \(\varphi^{(i)}\) on \(U_i\). So \(H\) is a sheaf and an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module.

(b) Here the map is \(\varphi\mapsto\varphi_X\), from \(\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})(X)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\) to \(\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)\), where \(M=\mathcal{F}(X)\), \(N=\mathcal{G}(X)\). By Thm. 1.7, \(\mathcal{F}\simeq\widetilde M\), so every section of \(\mathcal{F}\) over \(D(f)\) is of the form \(f^{-n}\,m|_{D(f)}\) with \(m\in M\).

Injective. Let \(\varphi_X=0\). For \(s=f^{-n}m|_{D(f)}\) we get \(\varphi_{D(f)}(s)=f^{-n}\varphi_{D(f)}(m|_{D(f)})=f^{-n}\varphi_X(m)|_{D(f)}=0\). If \(U\) is any open set and \(s\in\mathcal{F}(U)\), cover \(U\) by principal opens \(D(f)\); \(\varphi_U(s)\) vanishes on each, so \(\varphi_U(s)=0\). Thus \(\varphi=0\).

Surjective. Let \(u\in\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)\). For \(f\in A\) define \(u_f:M_f=\mathcal{F}(D(f))\to\mathcal{G}(D(f))\) by \(u_f(m/f^n)=f^{-n}u(m)|_{D(f)}\). It is well defined: if \(m/f^n=m'/f^{n'}\) in \(M_f\), then \(f^k(f^{n'}m-f^nm')=0\) in \(M\) for some \(k\), hence \(f^k(f^{n'}u(m)-f^nu(m'))=0\) in \(N\); restricting to \(D(f)\), where \(f\) is a unit, gives \(f^{-n}u(m)|=f^{-n'}u(m')|\). It is \(A_f\)-linear, and compatible with the restriction maps for \(D(g)\subseteq D(f)\) (all maps are induced by \(u\) and the localization maps). Since the principal opens form a base of the topology, the \(u_f\) glue to a morphism of sheaves \(\varphi:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}\), which is \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-linear (it is \(A_f\)-linear on each \(D(f)\) and linearity can be checked locally on sections). By construction \(\varphi_X=u_1=u\). ∎

Exercise 1.6

Problem

Let \(X\) be a locally Noetherian scheme, and let \(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\) be \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-modules. (a) Show that if \(\mathcal{F}\) is coherent and \(\mathcal{G}\) quasi-coherent, then \(\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\) is quasi-coherent (use Exercise 1.2.8). (b) Show that if \(\mathcal{F}\) and \(\mathcal{G}\) are coherent, then so is \(\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\).

Solution

(a) Quasi-coherence is local, so let \(X=\operatorname{Spec}A\) with \(A\) Noetherian. Then \(\mathcal{F}=\widetilde M\) with \(M=\mathcal{F}(X)\) finitely generated (Prop. 1.11), hence finitely presented since \(A\) is Noetherian, and \(\mathcal{G}=\widetilde N\), \(N=\mathcal{G}(X)\).

Algebraic fact (the content of Exercise 1.2.8). For \(f\in A\) the natural map \(\lambda_f:\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)_f\to\operatorname{Hom}_{A_f}(M_f,N_f)\), \((u/f^n)\mapsto(m/f^k\mapsto u(m)/f^{n+k})\) is an isomorphism when \(M\) is finitely presented. Indeed, take \(A^m\to A^n\to M\to0\) exact. Then \(0\to\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)\to N^n\to N^m\) is exact; localizing (exact) gives \(0\to\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)_f\to N_f^n\to N_f^m\) exact. Also \(A_f^m\to A_f^n\to M_f\to0\) is exact, so \(0\to\operatorname{Hom}_{A_f}(M_f,N_f)\to N_f^n\to N_f^m\) is exact, and \(\lambda_f\) is compatible with the maps to \(N_f^n\) and \(N_f^m\) (these being identities). Hence \(\lambda_f\) is an isomorphism (both are the kernel of the same map).

By Ex. 1.5(b) applied to the affine scheme \(D(f)=\operatorname{Spec}A_f\) and the quasi-coherent sheaf \(\mathcal{F}|_{D(f)}=(M_f)^\sim\), we have \(\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})(D(f))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{A_f}(M_f,N_f)\) via \(\varphi\mapsto\varphi_{D(f)}\), compatibly with restriction to \(D(g)\subseteq D(f)\). Combining with \(\lambda_f\), we get isomorphisms \(\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)_f\simeq\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})(D(f))\) compatible with restrictions, so \(\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)^\sim\), which is quasi-coherent (Prop. 1.5(c)).

(b) By (a), \(\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\) is quasi-coherent. Let \(U=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be an affine open subset with \(A\) Noetherian. Then \(\mathcal{H}(U)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)\) with \(M=\mathcal{F}(U)\), \(N=\mathcal{G}(U)\) finitely generated. If \(M\) is generated by \(n\) elements, \(\operatorname{Hom}_A(M,N)\) embeds in \(N^n\) and is a finitely generated module, \(A\) being Noetherian. The same argument applies to every affine open \(U\) of \(X\) (its ring is Noetherian), so \(\mathcal{H}\) is quasi-coherent with \(\mathcal{H}(U)\) finitely generated for all affine \(U\), i.e. (iii) of Prop. 1.11 holds; since \(X\) is locally Noetherian, \(\mathcal{H}\) is coherent by that proposition. ∎

Exercise 1.7

Problem

Let \(X\) be a scheme. Show that any finitely generated sheaf of ideals \(\mathcal{J}\) is quasi-coherent. If \(X\) is locally Noetherian, show that any quasi-coherent sheaf of ideals is coherent.

Solution

Let \(\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_X\) be a finitely generated sheaf of ideals and \(x\in X\). By Def. 1.10 there are an open neighborhood \(U\) of \(x\) and a surjection \(\pi:\mathcal{O}_U^n\to\mathcal{J}|_U\). Let \(\iota:\mathcal{J}|_U\to\mathcal{O}_U\) be the inclusion. Then \(\iota\circ\pi:\mathcal{O}_U^n\to\mathcal{O}_U\) is a morphism of quasi-coherent sheaves whose image is \(\mathcal{J}|_U\) (the image sheaf of \(\iota\pi\) is the image of \(\iota\) since \(\pi\) is surjective, i.e. \(\mathcal{J}|_U\)). By Prop. 1.12(c), \(\mathcal{J}|_U\) is quasi-coherent. Quasi-coherence being local (Def. 1.4), \(\mathcal{J}\) is quasi-coherent.

Now let \(X\) be locally Noetherian and \(\mathcal{J}\) a quasi-coherent sheaf of ideals. For an affine open \(U\), \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)\) is Noetherian and \(\mathcal{J}(U)\) is an ideal of it, hence finitely generated. So \(\mathcal{J}\) is quasi-coherent with \(\mathcal{J}(U)\) finitely generated for every affine open \(U\), i.e. property (iii) of Prop. 1.11 holds, and by that proposition (X locally Noetherian) \(\mathcal{J}\) is coherent. ∎

Exercise 1.8

Problem

Let \(X\) be a scheme. Let \(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\) be two quasi-coherent subsheaves of an \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module \(\mathcal{H}\). Show that \(\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{G}\) and \(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}\) are quasi-coherent, where \(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}\) is the sheaf associated to the presheaf \(U\mapsto\mathcal{F}(U)+\mathcal{G}(U)\). Show that these properties are true for coherent sheaves if \(X\) is locally Noetherian.

Solution

The statement is false as printed when \(\mathcal{H}\) is an arbitrary \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module, and it is true when \(\mathcal{H}\) is quasi-coherent (which is surely what is intended). We give the counterexample and then prove the corrected statement, including the coherent version.

Counterexample. Let \(X=\operatorname{Spec}R\) for a discrete valuation ring \(R\) with fraction field \(K\), so \(X=\{\xi,s\}\) with generic point \(\xi\) and closed point \(s\), and the opens are \(\emptyset,\{\xi\},X\). Let \(\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_X\) be given by \(\mathcal{J}(X)=0\), \(\mathcal{J}(\{\xi\})=K=\mathcal{O}_X(\{\xi\})\), \(\mathcal{J}(\emptyset)=0\). This is a sheaf (any cover of \(X\) contains \(X\)) and a sheaf of ideals. It is not quasi-coherent: otherwise \(\mathcal{J}\simeq\mathcal{J}(X)^\sim=0\) by Thm. 1.7, but \(\mathcal{J}(\{\xi\})=K\neq0\). Let \(\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{O}_X^2/\mathcal{R}\) where \(\mathcal{R}=\{(a,-a): a\in\mathcal{J}\}\simeq\mathcal{J}\), and let \(\mathcal{F}\) (resp. \(\mathcal{G}\)) be the image in \(\mathcal{H}\) of \(\mathcal{O}_X\oplus0\) (resp. \(0\oplus\mathcal{O}_X\)). The map \(\mathcal{O}_X\to\mathcal{H}\), \(a\mapsto[(a,0)]\) is injective (\((a,0)\in\mathcal{R}\) forces \(a=0\)), so \(\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathcal{O}_X\), likewise \(\mathcal{G}\simeq\mathcal{O}_X\); both are coherent, hence quasi-coherent. At a point \(x\), \(\mathcal{F}_x\cap\mathcal{G}_x\) consists of the classes \([(a,0)]=[(0,b)]\), i.e. \((a,-b)\in\mathcal{R}_x\), i.e. \(a=b\in\mathcal{J}_x\). So \(\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{G}\) is the image of \(\mathcal{J}\) under the injection \(\mathcal{J}\to\mathcal{F}\), \(a\mapsto[(a,0)]\), hence \(\simeq\mathcal{J}\), which is not quasi-coherent. Also \(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{H}\), and \(\mathcal{H}\) is not quasi-coherent either: otherwise the kernel \(\mathcal{R}\) of \(\mathcal{O}_X^2\to\mathcal{H}\) would be quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.12(c). (So both claims fail for non-quasi-coherent \(\mathcal{H}\), and \(X\) is even Noetherian, so the coherent version fails as well.)

Corrected statement and proof. Let \(\mathcal{H}\) be quasi-coherent and \(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\subseteq\mathcal{H}\) quasi-coherent subsheaves. Consider \(u:\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}\), \((s,t)\mapsto s-t\). The sheaf \(\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{G}\) is quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.12(a), \(\mathcal{H}\) is quasi-coherent, so \(\operatorname{Ker}u\) and \(\operatorname{Im}u\) are quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.12(c). Now \(\operatorname{Ker}u=\{(s,s)\}\simeq\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{G}\) via \((s,s)\mapsto s\), so \(\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{G}\) is quasi-coherent. And for \(v:\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}\), \((s,t)\mapsto s+t\), \(\operatorname{Im}v\) is the subsheaf of \(\mathcal{H}\) whose sections are those that are locally of the form \(s+t\), i.e. the sheaf associated to \(U\mapsto\mathcal{F}(U)+\mathcal{G}(U)\); thus \(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}=\operatorname{Im}v\) is quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.12(c).

Coherent case. Let \(X\) be locally Noetherian and \(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\) coherent subsheaves of a quasi-coherent (in particular, of a coherent) \(\mathcal{H}\). By the above, \(\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{G}\) and \(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}\) are quasi-coherent. For \(U\) affine, \((\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{G})(U)=\mathcal{F}(U)\cap\mathcal{G}(U)\) is a submodule of the finitely generated module \(\mathcal{F}(U)\) over the Noetherian ring \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)\), hence finitely generated; and \(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}=\operatorname{Im}v\) with \(v\) as above. Since \(\operatorname{Ker}v\) is quasi-coherent (Prop. 1.12(c)), Prop. 1.8 applied on the affine scheme \(U\) to \(0\to\operatorname{Ker}v\to\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{G}\to\operatorname{Im}v\to0\) shows that \((\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G})(U)\) is a quotient of \(\mathcal{F}(U)\oplus\mathcal{G}(U)\), hence finitely generated. By Prop. 1.11 both are coherent. (If \(\mathcal{H}\) is coherent this is also Prop. 1.12(e).) ∎

Exercise 1.9

Problem

Let \(\mathcal{F}\) be a coherent sheaf on a locally Noetherian scheme \(X\). (a) Show that the support \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{F}\) of \(\mathcal{F}\) (Exercise 2.2.5) is closed. (b) Show that the multiplication by scalars defines a canonical homomorphism of \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-modules \(\alpha:\mathcal{O}_X\to\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}_{\mathcal{O}_X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})\). Show that \(\operatorname{Ker}\alpha\) is quasi-coherent. We denote it by \(\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F}\). This sheaf is called the annihilator of \(\mathcal{F}\). (c) Show that for any affine open subset \(U\) of \(X\), we have \((\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})(U)=\{a\in\mathcal{O}_X(U)\mid a\mathcal{F}(U)=0\}\). (d) Show that \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{F}\) coincides with the closed subset \(V(\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})\). Show that \(\mathcal{F}\) is the direct image of a coherent sheaf on the closed subscheme \(V(\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})\) under the closed immersion \(V(\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})\to X\).

Solution

Here \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{F}=\{x\in X:\mathcal{F}_x\neq0\}\) and, for a quasi-coherent ideal \(\mathcal{J}\), \(V(\mathcal{J})=\{x:\mathcal{J}_x\neq\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\}\).

A preliminary algebraic fact. Let \(A\) be a ring, \(M\) a finitely generated \(A\)-module, \(\mathfrak{p}\) a prime. Then \(M_\mathfrak{p}\neq0\) iff \(\operatorname{Ann}_A(M)\subseteq\mathfrak{p}\); and \(\operatorname{Ann}_{A_\mathfrak{p}}(M_\mathfrak{p})=\operatorname{Ann}_A(M)_\mathfrak{p}\). Let \(m_1,\dots,m_r\) generate \(M\). If \(a/s\in\operatorname{Ann}(M_\mathfrak{p})\), then \(t_ia\,m_i=0\) for some \(t_i\notin\mathfrak{p}\); with \(t=\prod t_i\), \(ta\in\operatorname{Ann}_AM\) and \(a/s=ta/(ts)\in\operatorname{Ann}(M)_\mathfrak{p}\). The other inclusion is clear. Now \(M_\mathfrak{p}=0\) iff \(\operatorname{Ann}(M_\mathfrak{p})=A_\mathfrak{p}\) iff \(\operatorname{Ann}(M)_\mathfrak{p}=A_\mathfrak{p}\) iff \(\operatorname{Ann}(M)\not\subseteq\mathfrak{p}\).

(a) Let \(U=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be an affine open subset with \(A\) Noetherian. Then \(\mathcal{F}|_U=\widetilde M\) with \(M=\mathcal{F}(U)\) finitely generated (Prop. 1.11). By the fact above, \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{F}\cap U=\{\mathfrak{p}:M_\mathfrak{p}\neq0\}=V(\operatorname{Ann}_AM)\), closed in \(U\). Cover \(X\) by such \(U\); a subset whose intersection with each member of an open cover is closed is closed. So \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{F}\) is closed.

(b) For an open set \(U\) and \(a\in\mathcal{O}_X(U)\) let \(\alpha_U(a)\in\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})(U)\) be the endomorphism \(s\mapsto as\) of \(\mathcal{F}|_U\). It is an \(\mathcal{O}_X|_U\)-linear map by commutativity of \(\mathcal{O}_X(W)\), and \(a\mapsto\alpha_U(a)\) is \(\mathcal{O}_X(U)\)-linear and compatible with restrictions. So \(\alpha:\mathcal{O}_X\to\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})\) is a homomorphism of \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-modules. By Ex. 1.6(b), \(\mathcal{H}\!\operatorname{om}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})\) is coherent, so \(\alpha\) is a morphism of quasi-coherent sheaves and \(\operatorname{Ker}\alpha\) is quasi-coherent (indeed coherent) by Prop. 1.12(c),(e).

(c) By definition \((\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})(U)=\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_U=\{a\in\mathcal{O}_X(U): a\cdot s=0\text{ for all }W\subseteq U\text{ and }s\in\mathcal{F}(W)\}\). If \(a\) is in this set then \(a\mathcal{F}(U)=0\). Conversely let \(U=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be affine, \(M=\mathcal{F}(U)\) and \(aM=0\). For \(f\in A\), \(\mathcal{F}(D(f))=M_f\) and \(aM_f=0\). So \(a\) kills all sections over principal opens; since a section over any open set is locally a section over a principal open, \(a\cdot s=0\) for all sections, i.e. \(\alpha_U(a)=0\).

(d) Let \(U=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be affine, \(M=\mathcal{F}(U)\), \(I=\operatorname{Ann}_AM\). By (c), applied on every \(D(f)\), \((\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})(D(f))=\operatorname{Ann}_{A_f}(M_f)\), which equals \(I_f\) by the same argument as in the preliminary fact (\(M_f\) is finitely generated over \(A_f\)). Since \(\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F}\) is quasi-coherent, \((\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})|_U=\widetilde I\) (the two sheaves have the same sections on principal opens). Then for \(\mathfrak{p}\in U\): \(\mathfrak{p}\in V(\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})\) iff \(I_\mathfrak{p}\neq A_\mathfrak{p}\) iff \(I\subseteq\mathfrak{p}\) iff \(M_\mathfrak{p}\neq0\) iff \(\mathfrak{p}\in\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{F}\) (the preliminary fact). As such \(U\) cover \(X\), \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{F}=V(\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F})\).

Now let \(\mathcal{J}=\operatorname{Ann}\mathcal{F}\), a quasi-coherent (in fact coherent) sheaf of ideals, \(Z=V(\mathcal{J})\) with its closed subscheme structure (Prop. 1.15, Rem. 1.16), \(i:Z\to X\) the closed immersion; recall \(\mathcal{O}_Z=i^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_X/\mathcal{J})\), i.e. \(i_*\mathcal{O}_Z=\mathcal{O}_X/\mathcal{J}\). For every open \(U\), \(\mathcal{J}(U)=\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_U\) annihilates \(\mathcal{F}(U)\), so \(\mathcal{F}\) is a module over the presheaf of rings \(U\mapsto\mathcal{O}_X(U)/\mathcal{J}(U)\), hence over its sheafification \(\mathcal{O}_X/\mathcal{J}\). Then \(\mathcal{G}:=i^{-1}\mathcal{F}\) is a module over \(i^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_X/\mathcal{J})=\mathcal{O}_Z\). We have \(i_*\mathcal{G}=i_*i^{-1}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathcal{F}\) through the canonical map \(\mathcal{F}\to i_*i^{-1}\mathcal{F}\): on stalks, at \(x\in Z\) both sides are \(\mathcal{F}_x\) (as \(i\) is a homeomorphism onto the closed set \(Z\), \((i_*\mathcal{G})_x=\mathcal{G}_x=\mathcal{F}_x\) as in Ex. 1.1(a)), and at \(x\notin Z\) both are \(0\) (\(\mathcal{F}_x=0\) since \(x\notin\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{F}=Z\), and \((i_*\mathcal{G})_x=0\) because \(x\) has a neighborhood missing the closed set \(Z\)). The \(\mathcal{O}_X\)-module structures agree by construction.

\(\mathcal{G}\) is coherent on \(Z\): this is local, so take \(U=\operatorname{Spec}A\subseteq X\) affine with \(A\) Noetherian, \(\mathcal{F}|_U=\widetilde M\), \(I=\operatorname{Ann}_AM\). Then \(Z\cap U=\operatorname{Spec}(A/I)\) and \(M\) is an \(A/I\)-module, finitely generated. Let \(\mathcal{N}=\widetilde M\) on \(\operatorname{Spec}A/I\) (coherent, \(A/I\) Noetherian, Prop. 1.11). We have \(i_*\mathcal{N}(D(f))=\mathcal{N}(D(\bar f))=M_{\bar f}=M_f\), so \(i_*\mathcal{N}\simeq\mathcal{F}|_U\), and \(\mathcal{G}|_{Z\cap U}=i^{-1}\mathcal{F}|_U\simeq i^{-1}i_*\mathcal{N}\simeq\mathcal{N}\) (again by stalks: \((i^{-1}i_*\mathcal{N})_z=(i_*\mathcal{N})_{i(z)}=\mathcal{N}_z\)). Hence \(\mathcal{G}\) is coherent and \(\mathcal{F}=i_*\mathcal{G}\). ∎

Exercise 1.10

Problem

Let \(X\) be a scheme. (a) Let \(\mathcal{N}\) be the sheaf of nilpotent elements of \(\mathcal{O}_X\) (defined in the proof of Proposition 2.4.2). Show that it is quasi-coherent. (b) Let us suppose \(X\) locally Noetherian. Show that the set of \(x\in X\) such that \(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\) is reduced is open in \(X\). (c) Let \(\mathcal{I}\) be a quasi-coherent sheaf of ideals of \(\mathcal{O}_X\). Show that \(U\mapsto\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(U)}\), for every affine open \(U\), defines a quasi-coherent sheaf of ideals of \(\mathcal{O}_X\).

Solution

(a) Here \(\mathcal{N}(V)=\{s\in\mathcal{O}_X(V): s_x\text{ is nilpotent in }\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\text{ for all }x\in V\}\), a sheaf of ideals with stalk \(\mathcal{N}_x=\operatorname{Nil}(\mathcal{O}_{X,x})\). Let \(U=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be affine and \(f\in A\). We claim \(\mathcal{N}(D(f))=\operatorname{Nil}(A_f)=\operatorname{Nil}(A)_f\). If \(s\in\operatorname{Nil}(A_f)\) then every germ is nilpotent. Conversely if all germs \(s_\mathfrak{p}\) (\(\mathfrak{p}\in D(f)\), primes of \(A_f\)) are nilpotent, then \(s_\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{p}A_\mathfrak{p}\), so \(s\in\mathfrak{p}\) for all primes \(\mathfrak{p}\) of \(A_f\), so \(s\in\operatorname{Nil}(A_f)\). Finally \(\operatorname{Nil}(A_f)=\operatorname{Nil}(A)_f\): if \((a/f^k)^n=0\) then \(f^ma^n=0\) for some \(m\), so for \(N\ge n,m\) we get \((fa)^N=f^{N-m}a^{N-n}\cdot f^ma^n=0\); hence \(a/f^k=fa/f^{k+1}\in\operatorname{Nil}(A)_f\). The other inclusion is clear. These identifications are compatible with restriction (they are inclusions in \(A_f\subseteq\) ambient localizations), so \(\mathcal{N}|_U\simeq\operatorname{Nil}(A)^\sim\) (a morphism of sheaves given on principal opens), which is quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.5(c). Since affine opens cover \(X\), \(\mathcal{N}\) is quasi-coherent.

(b) By (a), \(\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_X\) is a quasi-coherent sheaf of ideals, hence coherent by Ex. 1.7 (\(X\) locally Noetherian). By Ex. 1.9(a), \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{N}\) is closed. Now \(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\) is reduced iff \(\mathcal{N}_x=\operatorname{Nil}(\mathcal{O}_{X,x})=0\) iff \(x\notin\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{N}\). So the set of \(x\) with \(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\) reduced is \(X\setminus\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{N}\), which is open.

(c) Define \(\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_X\) by \(\mathcal{J}(V)=\{s\in\mathcal{O}_X(V): s_x\in\sqrt{\mathcal{I}_x}\text{ for all }x\in V\}\), a sheaf of ideals (the condition is local on \(V\)). Let \(U=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be affine, \(I_0=\mathcal{I}(U)\subseteq A\). Since \(\mathcal{I}\) is quasi-coherent, \(\mathcal{I}|_U=\widetilde{I_0}\), so \(\mathcal{I}(D(f))=I_0A_f\) and \(\mathcal{I}_\mathfrak{p}=I_0A_\mathfrak{p}\). Claim: \(\mathcal{J}(D(f))=\sqrt{I_0A_f}\). If \(s^n\in I_0A_f\), then \(s_\mathfrak{p}^n\in I_0A_\mathfrak{p}\) for all \(\mathfrak{p}\in D(f)\), so \(s\in\mathcal{J}(D(f))\). Conversely if \(s\notin\sqrt{I_0A_f}\), there is a prime \(\mathfrak{q}\) of \(A_f\) containing \(I_0A_f\) with \(s\notin\mathfrak{q}\); then \(\sqrt{I_0A_\mathfrak{q}}\subseteq\mathfrak{q}A_\mathfrak{q}\not\ni s_\mathfrak{q}\), so \(s\notin\mathcal{J}(D(f))\). Next, \(\sqrt{I_0A_f}=(\sqrt{I_0})_f\): if \((a/f^k)^n\in I_0A_f\), then \(f^ma^n\in I_0\) for some \(m\), hence for \(N\ge n,m\), \((fa)^N=f^{N-m}a^{N-n}\cdot f^ma^n\in I_0\), so \(fa\in\sqrt{I_0}\) and \(a/f^k=fa/f^{k+1}\in(\sqrt{I_0})_f\); the converse is clear. So \(\mathcal{J}|_U\simeq(\sqrt{I_0})^\sim\) is quasi-coherent, and \(\mathcal{J}(U)=\sqrt{I_0}=\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(U)}\). Thus \(\mathcal{J}\) is a quasi-coherent sheaf of ideals with \(\mathcal{J}(U)=\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(U)}\) for every affine open \(U\). It is the only subsheaf of \(\mathcal{O}_X\) with this property, because a subsheaf of \(\mathcal{O}_X\) is determined by its sections over the affine opens (they form a base). ∎

Exercise 1.11

Problem

Let \(X\) be a locally Noetherian scheme, \(\varphi:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}\) a homomorphism of coherent sheaves. Show that \(\{x\in X\mid\varphi_x\text{ is an isomorphism}\}\) is an open subset of \(X\).

Solution

By Prop. 1.12(e), \(\mathcal{K}=\operatorname{Ker}\varphi\) and \(\mathcal{C}=\operatorname{Coker}\varphi\) are coherent. Taking stalks is exact, so \((\operatorname{Ker}\varphi)_x=\operatorname{Ker}\varphi_x\) and \((\operatorname{Coker}\varphi)_x=\operatorname{Coker}\varphi_x\). Hence \(\varphi_x\) is an isomorphism iff \(\mathcal{K}_x=0\) and \(\mathcal{C}_x=0\), i.e. iff \(x\notin\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{K}\cup\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal{C}\). By Ex. 1.9(a) both supports are closed. So the set in question is the complement of a closed set, hence open. ∎

Conventions for Exercises 1.12–1.22. Chapter and section numbers of the book are cited as in the text (“Prop. 1.14” is Prop. 1.14 of Section 5.1; “Lemma 1.2.7” is Nakayama's lemma, Chapter 1, Section 2). Stalks are written \(\mathcal F_x\), \(k(x)=\mathcal O_{X,x}/\fm_x\). Several solutions use the following standard facts. Fact A. If \(N\) is a finitely generated \(A\)-module, \(\fp\) a prime and \(N_\fp=0\), then \(N_f=0\) for some \(f\notin\fp\) (each of finitely many generators is killed by an element outside \(\fp\); take \(f\) to be their product). Fact B. A coherent sheaf is quasi-coherent: if \(\mathcal F\) is coherent and \(x\in X\), there are a neighbourhood \(U\) and a surjection \(\mathcal O_X^n|_U\to\mathcal F|_U\); its kernel is finitely generated, so after shrinking \(U\) there is a surjection \(\mathcal O_X^m|_U\to\operatorname{Ker}\), and \(\mathcal O_X^m|_U\to\mathcal O_X^n|_U\to\mathcal F|_U\to0\) is exact (Def. 1.4). Hence, if \(X\) is locally Noetherian and \(U=\Spec A\) is an affine open, a coherent \(\mathcal F\) satisfies \(\mathcal F|_U=\widetilde M\) with \(M=\mathcal F(U)\) a finitely generated \(A\)-module (Thm. 1.7, Prop. 1.11), and \(A\) is Noetherian. Finally, a morphism of sheaves is an isomorphism (resp. surjective) iff it is so on all stalks.

Exercise 1.12

Problem

Let \(X\) be a scheme. A sheaf \(\mathcal F\) on \(X\) is free of rank \(n\) if \(\mathcal F\simeq\mathcal O_X^n\). It is locally free of rank \(n\) if there is a covering \(\{X_i\}_i\) of \(X\) such that \(\mathcal F|_{X_i}\) is free of rank \(n\) for all \(i\), and locally free of finite rank if there are a covering \(\{X_i\}\) and integers \(n_i\) with \(\mathcal F|_{X_i}\) free of rank \(n_i\). Suppose \(X\) locally Noetherian.

(a) Let \(\mathcal F\) be a coherent sheaf on \(X\). Show that if \(\mathcal F_x\) is free of rank \(n\) over \(\mathcal O_{X,x}\), then there is an open neighbourhood \(U\) of \(x\) such that \(\mathcal F|_U\) is free of rank \(n\). Deduce that \(\mathcal F\) is locally free of rank \(n\) if and only if \(\mathcal F_x\) is free of rank \(n\) over \(\mathcal O_{X,x}\) for every \(x\in X\). In the remainder of the exercise \(\mathcal F\) satisfies this condition.

(b) Show that \(\mathcal F^\vee:=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal O_X}(\mathcal F,\mathcal O_X)\) is locally free of rank \(n\).

(c) Show that the canonical homomorphism \(\mathcal F\otimes_{\mathcal O_X}\mathcal F^\vee\to\mathcal O_X\) is an isomorphism if \(n=1\).

(d) Let \(\operatorname{Pic}(X)\) be the set of isomorphism classes of invertible sheaves on \(X\). Show that the tensor product makes \(\operatorname{Pic}(X)\) a commutative group with unit element the class of \(\mathcal O_X\), and that the inverse of the class of \(\mathcal L\) is the class of \(\mathcal L^\vee\).

Solution

(a) Let \(x\in X\) and choose an affine open neighbourhood \(V=\Spec A\) of \(x\), with \(A\) Noetherian. By Fact B, \(\mathcal F|_V=\widetilde M\) with \(M\) finitely generated; let \(\fp\subset A\) be the prime of \(x\). Since \(M_\fp=\mathcal F_x\) is free of rank \(n\), there are \(m_1,\dots,m_n\in M\) whose images form a basis of \(M_\fp\) (a basis consists of fractions \(m/a\), \(a\notin\fp\), and \(m/a\) and \(m/1\) differ by a unit). Define \(\varphi:A^n\to M\), \(e_i\mapsto m_i\). Then \(\varphi_\fp\) is an isomorphism. The kernel \(K\) and cokernel \(C\) of \(\varphi\) are finitely generated (\(A\) is Noetherian) and \(K_\fp=C_\fp=0\), so by Fact A there is \(f\notin\fp\) with \(K_f=C_f=0\). Hence \(\varphi_f:A_f^n\to M_f\) is an isomorphism, and by Prop. 1.5(b) the induced map \(\mathcal O_X^n|_U\to\mathcal F|_U\) on \(U=D(f)=\Spec A_f\) is an isomorphism. So \(\mathcal F|_U\) is free of rank \(n\).

If \(\mathcal F\) is locally free of rank \(n\), then \(\mathcal F_x\simeq\mathcal O_{X,x}^n\) for every \(x\) (take the stalk of \(\mathcal F|_{X_i}\simeq\mathcal O_{X_i}^n\) at \(x\in X_i\)). Conversely, if every \(\mathcal F_x\) is free of rank \(n\), the first part gives for every \(x\) an open \(U_x\ni x\) with \(\mathcal F|_{U_x}\) free of rank \(n\); these \(U_x\) cover \(X\).

(b) The question is local, and \(\mathcal F^\vee|_U=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal O_U}(\mathcal F|_U,\mathcal O_U)\) by definition of the sheaf Hom (Ex. 1.5(a)); it depends only on \(\mathcal F|_U\) up to isomorphism. So let \(\mathcal F|_U\simeq\mathcal O_U^n\). For every open \(V\subseteq U\), a homomorphism \(\mathcal O_V^n\to\mathcal O_V\) is determined by the images \(a_1,\dots,a_n\in\mathcal O_X(V)\) of the basis vectors, and any \((a_i)\) occurs. Thus \(\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal O_V^n,\mathcal O_V)\simeq\mathcal O_X(V)^n\), compatibly with restriction and \(\mathcal O_X(V)\)-module structure, i.e. \(\mathcal F^\vee|_U\simeq\mathcal O_U^n\).

(c) The canonical map \(\operatorname{ev}:\mathcal F\otimes\mathcal F^\vee\to\mathcal O_X\) is the one induced on the presheaf \(V\mapsto\mathcal F(V)\otimes_{\mathcal O_X(V)}\mathcal F^\vee(V)\) by \(u\otimes\psi\mapsto\psi(u)\), followed by sheafification. Being an isomorphism is local. Let \(\mathcal F|_U=\mathcal O_Ue\) with \(e\) a generator (so \(\mathcal F|_U\simeq\mathcal O_U\)). By (b), \(\mathcal F^\vee|_U=\mathcal O_Ue^*\), where \(e^*(e)=1\) and \(e^*\) is a generator. Then \(\mathcal F\otimes\mathcal F^\vee|_U\simeq\mathcal O_U\,(e\otimes e^*)\) is free of rank \(1\) with generator \(e\otimes e^*\), and \(\operatorname{ev}(e\otimes e^*)=e^*(e)=1\). Hence \(\operatorname{ev}|_U\) sends a generator of a free rank-one module to a generator of \(\mathcal O_U\), so it is an isomorphism.

(d) Here “invertible” means Def. 1.21: \(\mathcal L|_U\simeq\mathcal O_U\) locally. This is exactly “locally free of rank \(1\)” in the sense of the exercise. (For locally Noetherian \(X\), Def. 1.21 also records the equivalent stalk condition: \(\mathcal L\) coherent with \(\mathcal L_x\) free of rank \(1\) for all \(x\); this follows from (a) and from coherence of \(\mathcal O_X\), Prop. 1.11.) The tensor product of two invertible sheaves is invertible: \((\mathcal L\otimes\mathcal M)|_U\simeq\mathcal O_U\otimes\mathcal O_U\simeq\mathcal O_U\) on a common trivializing open. Hence \(\otimes\) induces a product on isomorphism classes (\(\mathcal L\simeq\mathcal L'\), \(\mathcal M\simeq\mathcal M'\) implies \(\mathcal L\otimes\mathcal M\simeq\mathcal L'\otimes\mathcal M'\)). It is associative and commutative because of the canonical isomorphisms \((\mathcal L\otimes\mathcal M)\otimes\mathcal N\simeq\mathcal L\otimes(\mathcal M\otimes\mathcal N)\) and \(\mathcal L\otimes\mathcal M\simeq\mathcal M\otimes\mathcal L\) of the tensor product of sheaves of modules. The class of \(\mathcal O_X\) is a unit because \(\mathcal O_X\otimes\mathcal L\simeq\mathcal L\). For an invertible \(\mathcal L\), \(\mathcal L^\vee\) is invertible by (b) (with \(n=1\)), and \(\mathcal L\otimes\mathcal L^\vee\simeq\mathcal O_X\) by (c). So \([\mathcal L^\vee]\) is the inverse of \([\mathcal L]\) (and inverses are unique in a monoid). ∎

Exercise 1.13

Problem

Let \(X\) be a locally Noetherian scheme and \(\varphi:\mathcal F\to\mathcal G\) a surjective morphism between two locally free sheaves of equal (finite) rank. Show that \(\varphi\) is an isomorphism (use Prop. 1.2.6).

Solution

“Equal rank” is read as: for every \(x\), \(\mathcal F_x\simeq\mathcal O_{X,x}^n\simeq\mathcal G_x\) with the same \(n=n(x)\). (This is automatic if both sheaves are locally free of the same constant rank \(n\).) The Noetherian hypothesis is not needed.

It suffices to show that every stalk map \(\varphi_x:\mathcal F_x\to\mathcal G_x\) is injective, since \(\varphi\) is already surjective. Put \(R=\mathcal O_{X,x}\), with maximal ideal \(\fm\) and residue field \(k\). Then \(\varphi_x:R^n\to R^n\) is a surjective \(R\)-linear map; let \(K\) be its kernel. As the target \(R^n\) is free, the exact sequence \(0\to K\to R^n\to R^n\to0\) splits, so \(R^n\simeq K\oplus R^n\), and \(K\), a quotient of \(R^n\), is finitely generated. Tensor with \(k\): since the sequence is exact with flat (free) third term, it stays exact by Prop. 1.2.6, giving \(0\to K\otimes k\to k^n\to k^n\to0\). Comparing dimensions, \(K\otimes_Rk=0\), i.e. \(K=\fm K\). By Nakayama's lemma (Lemma 1.2.7) \(K=0\). Hence \(\varphi_x\) is bijective for all \(x\), and \(\varphi\) is an isomorphism. ∎

Exercise 1.14

Problem

Let \(X\) be an integral scheme and \(\mathcal F\) a quasi-coherent sheaf on \(X\).

(a) Let \(\mathcal F(U)_{\operatorname{tors}}\) be the set of \(x\in\mathcal F(U)\) such that \(ax=0\) for some non-zero \(a\in\mathcal O_X(U)\). Show that it is a submodule of \(\mathcal F(U)\).

(b) Show that there exists a unique subsheaf \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\) of \(\mathcal F\) such that \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}(U)=\mathcal F(U)_{\operatorname{tors}}\) for every affine open subset \(U\) of \(X\). Show that \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\) is quasi-coherent.

(c) \(\mathcal F\) is torsion-free if \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}=0\). Show that \(\mathcal F/\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\) is always torsion-free.

(d) Suppose moreover that \(X\) is locally Noetherian and that \(\mathcal F\) is coherent and non-zero. Show that \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal F=X\) if and only if \(\mathcal F\neq\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\). Show that there exist \(n\ge0\) and a non-empty open subset \(U\) of \(X\) such that \(\mathcal O_X^n|_U\simeq\mathcal F|_U\) (by convention \(\mathcal O_X^n=\mathcal O_X\) if \(n=0\)).

Solution

Recall that on an integral scheme \(\mathcal O_X(U)\) is an integral domain for every non-empty open \(U\), and for non-empty opens \(V\subseteq U\) the restriction \(\mathcal O_X(U)\to\mathcal O_X(V)\) is injective. Hence restrictions of non-zero elements are non-zero. For \(U=\emptyset\) we set \(\mathcal F(\emptyset)_{\operatorname{tors}}=0=\mathcal F(\emptyset)\).

(a) Let \(U\neq\emptyset\). If \(ax=0\), \(by=0\) with \(a,b\neq0\), then \(ab\neq0\) (domain) and \(ab(x+y)=0\); and \(a(cx)=c(ax)=0\) for all \(c\in\mathcal O_X(U)\). Also \(0\in\mathcal F(U)_{\operatorname{tors}}\) (take \(a=1\)). So \(\mathcal F(U)_{\operatorname{tors}}\) is a submodule. Moreover, if \(x\in\mathcal F(U)_{\operatorname{tors}}\) and \(V\subseteq U\) is non-empty open, then \(x|_V\in\mathcal F(V)_{\operatorname{tors}}\) (use \(a|_V\neq0\)).

(b) A local-to-global remark. Let \(V=\Spec R\) be affine, \(s\in\mathcal F(V)\), and suppose \(V\) is covered by opens \(V_l\) with \(s|_{V_l}\) torsion. Then \(s\) is torsion. Indeed, refining we may take \(V_l=D(g_l)\) principal, finitely many (as \(V\) is quasi-compact), non-empty. If \(a_ls|_{D(g_l)}=0\) with \(0\neq a_l\in R_{g_l}\), write \(a_l=b_l/g_l^N\) with \(b_l\in R\); \(b_l\neq0\) since \(R\to R_{g_l}\) is injective for the domain \(R\) and \(a_l\ne 0\). Then \(b_ls|_{D(g_l)}=0\), and \(b=\prod_lb_l\neq0\) satisfies \(bs|_{D(g_l)}=0\) for all \(l\), so \(bs=0\).

Definition and uniqueness. For any open \(U\) set \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}(U)=\{s\in\mathcal F(U):s|_V\in\mathcal F(V)_{\operatorname{tors}}\text{ for all affine open }V\subseteq U\}\). By (a) this is a submodule; it is stable under restriction, and it is a sheaf: if \(s\in\mathcal F(U)\) and \(s|_{U_i}\in\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}(U_i)\) for a cover \(\{U_i\}\), then for an affine \(V\subseteq U\) the sets \(V\cap U_i\) are covered by affine opens \(V'\) with \(s|_{V'}\) torsion, so \(s|_V\) is torsion by the remark; and the sheaf axioms are inherited from \(\mathcal F\). If \(U\) is affine, then \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}(U)=\mathcal F(U)_{\operatorname{tors}}\): “\(\subseteq\)” take \(V=U\); “\(\supseteq\)” follows from the last sentence of (a). Conversely, if \(\mathcal H\subseteq\mathcal F\) is any subsheaf with \(\mathcal H(V)=\mathcal F(V)_{\operatorname{tors}}\) for all affine \(V\), then for any open \(U\), \(\mathcal H(U)=\{s\in\mathcal F(U):s|_V\in\mathcal H(V)\ \forall\text{ affine }V\subseteq U\}\) (affines cover \(U\) and \(\mathcal H\) is a subsheaf), which is \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}(U)\). This proves existence and uniqueness.

Quasi-coherence. Let \(U=\Spec A\) be affine, \(M=\mathcal F(U)\), so \(\mathcal F|_U=\widetilde M\) (Thm. 1.7), and \(N=M_{\operatorname{tors}}\). For \(0\neq g\in A\) we claim \((M_g)_{\operatorname{tors}}=N_g\) inside \(M_g\). The inclusion \(\supseteq\) is clear (\(am=0\) gives \((a/1)(m/g^k)=0\), \(a/1\neq0\)). For \(\subseteq\): if \((a/g^j)(m/g^k)=0\) with \(a/g^j\neq0\), then \(a\neq0\) and \(g^Nam=0\) for some \(N\); \(g^Na\neq0\) since \(A\) is a domain, so \(m\in N\). Now \(\widetilde N\subseteq\widetilde M=\mathcal F|_U\) is a subsheaf (Prop. 1.5(b)) with \(\widetilde N(D(g))=N_g\), and \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}(D(g))=(M_g)_{\operatorname{tors}}=N_g\) (principal opens are affine). Two subsheaves of \(\mathcal F|_U\) having the same sections on all principal opens coincide (principal opens form a base). Hence \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}|_U=\widetilde N\), which is quasi-coherent (Prop. 1.5(c)); as quasi-coherence is local, \(\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\) is quasi-coherent.

(c) \(\mathcal Q=\mathcal F/\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\) is quasi-coherent by Prop. 1.12(c). For \(U=\Spec A\) affine, with \(M,N\) as above, Prop. 1.5(b) gives \(\mathcal Q|_U=(M/N)^\sim\) and \(\mathcal Q(U)=M/N\). If \(a\bar m=0\) in \(M/N\) with \(a\neq0\), then \(am\in N\), so \(bam=0\) for some \(b\neq0\); as \(ba\neq0\) we get \(m\in N\). So \(\mathcal Q(U)_{\operatorname{tors}}=0\) for every affine \(U\), whence \(\mathcal Q_{\operatorname{tors}}=0\) (a subsheaf vanishing on all affine opens vanishes).

(d) Let \(\xi\) be the generic point. Claim 1: \(\operatorname{Supp}\mathcal F=X\) iff \(\mathcal F_\xi\neq0\). Only “\(\Leftarrow\)” needs proof. Let \(y\in X\); \(\xi\) generizes \(y\), so in an affine neighbourhood \(\Spec A\) of \(y\) the point \(\xi\) is the prime \((0)\) and \(y=\fq\), and \(M_{(0)}\) is a localization of \(M_\fq\). If \(M_\fq=0\) then \(M_{(0)}=0\). Hence \(\mathcal F_\xi\neq0\) implies \(\mathcal F_y\ne0\) for all \(y\).

Claim 2: \(\mathcal F_\xi\neq0\) iff \(\mathcal F\neq\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\). Let \(U=\Spec A\neq\emptyset\) be affine, \(M=\mathcal F(U)\), \(N=M_{\operatorname{tors}}\), \(K=\operatorname{Frac}A\). Then \(\mathcal F_\xi=M\otimes_AK\). Since \(N\otimes K=0\) (every element of \(N\) is killed by a non-zero element), exactness of \(\otimes K\) gives \(M\otimes K=(M/N)\otimes K\); and \(M/N\) is torsion-free (proof of (c)), so it embeds in \((M/N)\otimes K\). Hence \(\mathcal F_\xi\neq0\) iff \(M\neq N\). Now \(\mathcal F=\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\) iff \(M=N\) for every affine \(U\), iff \(\mathcal F_\xi=0\) (the condition \(M=N\) for one non-empty affine \(U\) is equivalent to \(\mathcal F_\xi=0\), which does not depend on \(U\)). This proves Claim 2, and with Claim 1 the first assertion.

Free on an open set. Let \(U=\Spec A\) be a non-empty affine open, \(A\) a Noetherian domain, \(M=\mathcal F(U)\) finitely generated (Fact B). The \(K\)-vector space \(M\otimes K\) is finite-dimensional, say of dimension \(n\ge0\), and is spanned by the images of elements of \(M\), so we can choose \(m_1,\dots,m_n\in M\) whose images form a basis. Let \(\varphi:A^n\to M\), \(e_i\mapsto m_i\). Then \(\varphi\otimes K\) is an isomorphism, so \(\operatorname{Ker}\varphi\otimes K=0=\operatorname{Coker}\varphi\otimes K\) (\(K\) is flat). These modules are finitely generated, so by Fact A (applied to the prime \((0)\)) there is \(0\neq f\in A\) killing both. Then \(\varphi_f\) is an isomorphism, so \(\mathcal O_X^n|_{D(f)}\simeq\mathcal F|_{D(f)}\) with \(D(f)\neq\emptyset\) (as \(f\neq0\) in a domain).

Remark on the convention. If \(\mathcal F\neq\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\) then \(\mathcal F_\xi\neq0\), so \(n\ge1\) and the convention for \(n=0\) is irrelevant. If \(\mathcal F=\mathcal F_{\operatorname{tors}}\) then \(n=0\) and the proof gives \(\mathcal F|_U=0\). In that case the statement must be read with \(\mathcal O_X^0=0\); with the book's convention \(\mathcal O_X^0=\mathcal O_X\) it would be false (e.g. \(X=\mathbb A^1_k\), \(\mathcal F\) the skyscraper sheaf \(k(0)\) at the origin, which is coherent and torsion; \(\mathcal F|_U=0\not\simeq\mathcal O_U\) for \(U=X\setminus\{0\}\), and for any non-empty open \(U\) the restriction \(\mathcal F|_U\) is \(0\) or a skyscraper, never \(\simeq\mathcal O_U\)). The assertion is proved as stated with \(\mathcal O_X^0=0\). ∎

Exercise 1.15

Problem

Let \(\mathcal F\) be a coherent sheaf on a locally Noetherian scheme \(X\). For \(x\in X\) set \(\phi(x)=\dim_{k(x)}(\mathcal F_x\otimes_{\mathcal O_{X,x}}k(x))\).

(a) Let \(n\in\mathbb N\). Using Nakayama's lemma, show that \(\{x\in X\mid\phi(x)\le n\}\) is open in \(X\). In particular, if \(X\) is irreducible with generic point \(\xi\), then \(\phi(x)\ge\phi(\xi)\) for all \(x\in X\).

(b) Let \(A\) be a ring and \(\alpha:A^n\to M\) a homomorphism of \(A\)-modules. Suppose that for every \(\fp\in\Spec A\) the homomorphism \(\alpha\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{k(\fp)}:k(\fp)^n\to M_\fp\otimes_{A_\fp}k(\fp)\) is injective. Show that \(\operatorname{Ker}\alpha\subseteq\mathcal N^n\), where \(\mathcal N\) is the nilradical of \(A\).

(c) Suppose \(\phi\) is constant of value \(n\) on \(X\) and \(X\) is reduced. Show that \(\mathcal F\) is locally free of rank \(n\).

(d) Show by an example that (c) is false if \(X\) is not reduced.

Solution

(a) Let \(x\in X\) with \(\phi(x)=r\le n\). Take an affine open \(U=\Spec A\ni x\), \(A\) Noetherian, \(\mathcal F|_U=\widetilde M\) with \(M\) finitely generated (Fact B), and let \(\fp\) be the prime of \(x\). The \(k(\fp)\)-vector space \(M_\fp/\fp M_\fp\) has dimension \(r\); choose \(m_1,\dots,m_r\in M\) whose images form a basis. By Nakayama (Lemma 1.2.7, for the finitely generated \(A_\fp\)-module \(M_\fp\) and the submodule generated by the \(m_i\)), \(m_1,\dots,m_r\) generate \(M_\fp\). Let \(C\) be the cokernel of \(A^r\to M\), \(e_i\mapsto m_i\); it is finitely generated and \(C_\fp=0\), so \(C_f=0\) for some \(f\notin\fp\) (Fact A). For \(y=\fq\in D(f)\), \(M_\fq\) is generated by the images of the \(m_i\), so \(\dim M_\fq/\fq M_\fq\le r\le n\). Hence \(D(f)\subseteq\{\phi\le n\}\) is a neighbourhood of \(x\), and \(\{\phi\le n\}\) is open.

If \(X\) is irreducible with generic point \(\xi\) and \(x\in X\), put \(n=\phi(x)\). The open set \(\{\phi\le n\}\) contains \(x\), hence is non-empty, hence contains \(\xi\) (the generic point lies in every non-empty open set). So \(\phi(\xi)\le n=\phi(x)\).

(b) Let \(v=(a_1,\dots,a_n)\in\operatorname{Ker}\alpha\subseteq A^n\) and \(\fp\in\Spec A\). The image \(\bar v\in k(\fp)^n\) of \(v\) satisfies \((\alpha\otimes\operatorname{Id})(\bar v)=\overline{\alpha(v)}=0\), so \(\bar v=0\) by injectivity, i.e. \(a_i\in\fp A_\fp\cap A=\fp\) for all \(i\). As \(\fp\) was arbitrary, \(a_i\in\bigcap_\fp\fp=\mathcal N\). (No finiteness hypothesis is needed.)

(c) Let \(x\in X\) and \(U=\Spec A\), \(M\), \(\fp\) as in (a), now with \(r=\phi(x)=n\). As in (a) there are \(m_1,\dots,m_n\in M\) and \(f\notin\fp\) such that \(\alpha:A^n\to M\), \(e_i\mapsto m_i\), becomes surjective after inverting \(f\). Replace \(A,M\) by \(A_f,M_f\); \(A_f\) is reduced because \(X\) is reduced. For every prime \(\fq\) of \(A_f\), the map \(\alpha\otimes k(\fq):k(\fq)^n\to M_\fq\otimes k(\fq)\) is surjective (right exactness of \(\otimes\)) between spaces of the same dimension \(n=\phi(\fq)\), hence injective. By (b), \(\operatorname{Ker}\alpha\subseteq\mathcal N^n=0\). So \(\alpha\) is an isomorphism, i.e. \(\mathcal F|_{D(f)}\simeq\mathcal O^n_{D(f)}\) (Prop. 1.5(b)). Thus \(\mathcal F\) is locally free of rank \(n\).

(d) Let \(k\) be a field, \(A=k[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)\), \(X=\Spec A\) (one point, not reduced), \(M=A/\varepsilon A\simeq k\) and \(\mathcal F=\widetilde M\). It is coherent (Prop. 1.11), \(\phi\) is the constant \(\dim_kM/\varepsilon M=1\) on the one point of \(X\), but \(\mathcal F\) is not locally free: the only non-empty open set is \(X\), and \(M\not\simeq A^n\) for any \(n\) because \(\varepsilon M=0\) whereas \(\varepsilon A^n\ne0\) for \(n\ge1\), and \(M\ne0\). ∎

Exercise 1.16

Problem

Let \(f:X\to S\) be a morphism of schemes, and consider a base change Here \(X_T=X\times_ST\), \(f_T:X_T\to T\) and \(p:X_T\to X\) are the projections, \(\pi:T\to S\) is the given morphism, and \(f\circ p=\pi\circ f_T\). (a) Let \(\mathcal F\) be an arbitrary \(\mathcal O_X\)-module. Show that there is a canonical homomorphism \(\pi^*f_*\mathcal F\to(f_T)_*p^*\mathcal F\) (use Exercise 1.1).

(b) Suppose \(T\to S\) is flat and \(\mathcal F\) is quasi-coherent. Show that \(\pi^*f_*\mathcal F\to(f_T)_*p^*\mathcal F\) is an isomorphism if \(X\) is Noetherian or if \(f\) is separated and quasi-compact (see also Prop. 3.1.24).

(c) Let \(X\) and \(f\) be as in (b), and let \(\mathcal G\) be a locally free sheaf on \(S\). Show that there is a canonical isomorphism \(f_*\mathcal F\otimes_{\mathcal O_S}\mathcal G\simeq f_*(\mathcal F\otimes_{\mathcal O_X}f^*\mathcal G)\) (see also Prop. 2.32).

Solution

The adjunction of Exercise 1.1(b). For a morphism \(g:Y\to Z\) of schemes, an \(\mathcal O_Z\)-module \(\mathcal G\) and an \(\mathcal O_Y\)-module \(\mathcal H\) there is a natural bijection \(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal O_Y}(g^*\mathcal G,\mathcal H)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal O_Z}(\mathcal G,g_*\mathcal H)\). Indeed, \(g^*\mathcal G=g^{-1}\mathcal G\otimes_{g^{-1}\mathcal O_Z}\mathcal O_Y\), so \(\mathcal O_Y\)-linear maps \(g^*\mathcal G\to\mathcal H\) correspond to \(g^{-1}\mathcal O_Z\)-linear maps \(g^{-1}\mathcal G\to\mathcal H\) (extension of scalars), and these correspond to maps of sheaves \(\mathcal G\to g_*\mathcal H\) by the adjunction \(g^{-1}\dashv g_*\) of sheaves of sets; linearity over \(g^{-1}\mathcal O_Z\) translates into \(\mathcal O_Z\)-linearity. In particular there are unit maps \(\eta:\mathcal G\to g_*g^*\mathcal G\), sending a section \(t\) to the section \(g^*t:=t\otimes1\).

(a) Since \(f\circ p=\pi\circ f_T\), we have \(\pi_*(f_T)_*p^*\mathcal F=f_*p_*p^*\mathcal F\). Let \(\theta:\pi^*f_*\mathcal F\to(f_T)_*p^*\mathcal F\) be the map adjoint (under \(\pi^*\dashv\pi_*\)) to the composite

\[ f_*\mathcal F\xrightarrow{\ f_*(\eta_{\mathcal F})\ }f_*p_*p^*\mathcal F=\pi_*(f_T)_*p^*\mathcal F. \]

Unwinding: if \(V\subseteq S\), \(W\subseteq T\) are open with \(\pi(W)\subseteq V\), \(s\in\mathcal F(f^{-1}V)\) and \(a\in\mathcal O_T(W)\), then \(\theta\) sends \(a\cdot\pi^*s|_W\) (the section of \(\pi^*f_*\mathcal F\) over \(W\) attached to \(s\)) to \(a\cdot(p^*s)|_{f_T^{-1}(W)}\in(p^*\mathcal F)(f_T^{-1}W)\); note \(f_T^{-1}(W)\subseteq p^{-1}f^{-1}(V)\).

(b) The statement is local on \(T\), so we may restrict to an affine open \(W=\Spec A'\subseteq T\) whose image lies in an affine open \(V=\Spec A\subseteq S\). Replace \(X\) by \(X_V=f^{-1}(V)\) (still Noetherian, resp. with \(f\) separated quasi-compact); then \(f_T^{-1}(W)=X_V\times_VW\), which we call \(X_W\). \(A'\) is flat over \(A\), and so is every localization \(A'_{g'}\) (\(g'\in A'\)).

Step 1: \(X_V\) has a finite affine cover \(\{U_i\}\) such that each \(U_i\cap U_j\) is covered by finitely many affine opens \(U_{ijk}\). If \(X_V\) is Noetherian, every open subset is quasi-compact. If \(f\) is separated and quasi-compact, \(X_V\) is quasi-compact and \(U_i\cap U_j\) is affine for affine \(U_i,U_j\) (separatedness over the affine \(V\)).

Step 2. Put \(M=\mathcal F(X_V)\) and let \(B_i=\mathcal O_X(U_i)\) etc. For any flat \(A\)-algebra \(A''\) (we shall use \(A''=A'_{g'}\), including \(g'=1\)) let \(X''=X_V\times_V\Spec A''\) and \(p'':X''\to X_V\). For an affine open \(U=\Spec B\subseteq X_V\), \(p''^{-1}(U)=\Spec(B\otimes_AA'')\) is affine and by Prop. 1.14(b) \(p''^*\mathcal F|_{p''^{-1}U}=(\mathcal F(U)\otimes_B(B\otimes_AA''))^\sim\), so \((p''^*\mathcal F)(p''^{-1}U)=\mathcal F(U)\otimes_AA''\), the identification being the map \(s\otimes a\mapsto a\cdot p''^*s\). The sets \(p''^{-1}(U_i)\) cover \(X''\) and \(p''^{-1}(U_i)\cap p''^{-1}(U_j)=\bigcup_kp''^{-1}(U_{ijk})\). The sheaf axiom for \(p''^*\mathcal F\) and for \(\mathcal F\) gives two exact sequences connected by natural maps

\begin{align*} 0\to M\otimes_AA''&\to\bigoplus_i\mathcal F(U_i)\otimes_AA''\to\bigoplus_{i,j,k}\mathcal F(U_{ijk})\otimes_AA'',\\ 0\to(p''^*\mathcal F)(X'')&\to\bigoplus_i(p''^*\mathcal F)(p''^{-1}U_i)\to\bigoplus_{i,j,k}(p''^*\mathcal F)(p''^{-1}U_{ijk}). \end{align*}

The first is obtained from \(0\to M\to\bigoplus\mathcal F(U_i)\to\bigoplus\mathcal F(U_{ijk})\) (exact by the sheaf axiom, \((s_i)\mapsto(s_i|-s_j|)\)) by tensoring with the flat \(A\)-module \(A''\). The second and third vertical maps are isomorphisms by the above, so by the four lemma the first vertical map \(M\otimes_AA''\to(p''^*\mathcal F)(X'')\) is an isomorphism.

Step 3. Now \(f_*\mathcal F|_V\) is quasi-coherent (Prop. 1.14(c), applied to \(X_V\to V\)), hence \(=\widetilde M\) with \(M=(f_*\mathcal F)(V)=\mathcal F(X_V)\) (Thm. 1.7). By Prop. 1.14(b), \((\pi^*f_*\mathcal F)|_W=(M\otimes_AA')^\sim\). On the other hand, for a principal open \(D(g')\subseteq W\), \(((f_T)_*p^*\mathcal F)(D(g'))=(p^*\mathcal F)(X_{D(g')})\), which by Step 2 for \(A''=A'_{g'}\) equals \(M\otimes_AA'_{g'}=(M\otimes_AA')_{g'}\); by the description of \(\theta\) in (a), \(\theta\) is on \(D(g')\) the identification of Step 2. Thus \(\theta|_W\) is an isomorphism on all principal opens, which form a base of \(W\), hence is an isomorphism of sheaves. (We did not need that \((f_T)_*p^*\mathcal F\) is quasi-coherent a priori; it follows.)

(c) The map. For \(V\subseteq S\) open, \(s\in\mathcal F(f^{-1}V)=(f_*\mathcal F)(V)\) and \(g\in\mathcal G(V)\), put \(f^*g:=\eta(g)|_{f^{-1}V}\) and consider \(s\otimes f^*g\in(\mathcal F\otimes f^*\mathcal G)(f^{-1}V)\). For \(a\in\mathcal O_S(V)\), \((as)\otimes f^*g=s\otimes f^*(ag)\) (both equal \(f^\#(a)\,(s\otimes f^*g)\)). So this defines a map of presheaves \(V\mapsto(f_*\mathcal F)(V)\otimes_{\mathcal O_S(V)}\mathcal G(V)\to f_*(\mathcal F\otimes f^*\mathcal G)(V)\); as the target is a sheaf, it factors through the sheafification and gives \(\mu:f_*\mathcal F\otimes\mathcal G\to f_*(\mathcal F\otimes f^*\mathcal G)\). It is functorial in \(\mathcal G\) and compatible with finite direct sums in \(\mathcal G\), and for \(\mathcal G=\mathcal O_S\) it is the identity of \(f_*\mathcal F\) (as \(f^*\mathcal O_S=\mathcal O_X\)).

It is an isomorphism. This is local on \(S\); on an open \(V\) with \(\mathcal G|_V\simeq\mathcal O_V^n\), functoriality and additivity reduce \(\mu|_V\) to the case \(\mathcal O_V^n\), where it is the identity of \((f_*\mathcal F|_V)^n\) (both sides equal \((f_*\mathcal F|_V)^n\), because \(f^*\) and \(\otimes\) commute with finite direct sums and \(f_*\) also does). Here “locally free” means locally free of finite rank, as in Ex. 1.12. The hypotheses on \(X\), \(f\) and the quasi-coherence of \(\mathcal F\) are therefore not needed for this statement; they are only needed for \(f_*\mathcal F\) to be quasi-coherent, so that the formula stays inside the world of quasi-coherent sheaves (Prop. 1.14(c)).

Remark (locally free of infinite rank). If \(\mathcal G|_V\simeq\mathcal O_V^{(I)}\) with \(I\) infinite, the hypotheses of (b) do give the result. They imply that \(f^{-1}(W)\) is quasi-compact for every affine open \(W\subseteq S\) (\(X\) Noetherian, resp. \(f\) quasi-compact). The sections of a direct sum of sheaves over a quasi-compact open set are the direct sum of the sections (see Ex. 1.18(a) below). Hence for affine \(W\subseteq V\) both \((f_*\mathcal F)^{(I)}(W)\) and \(f_*(\mathcal F^{(I)})(W)\) equal \(\mathcal F(f^{-1}W)^{(I)}\), and \(\mu|_V\) is an isomorphism on a base of the topology. ∎

Exercise 1.17

Problem

Let \(X\) be a scheme and \(\mathcal A\) a sheaf of (commutative) \(\mathcal O_X\)-algebras. We say that \(\mathcal A\) is quasi-coherent if it is quasi-coherent as an \(\mathcal O_X\)-module.

(a) Suppose \(\mathcal A\) quasi-coherent. Show that there exists a unique \(X\)-scheme \(\pi:Z\to X\) with \(\pi\) affine and \(\pi_*\mathcal O_Z=\mathcal A\). We denote \(Z\) by \(\Spec\mathcal A\).

(b) Show that \(\Spec\mathcal A\to X\) is finite if and only if \(\mathcal A\) is quasi-coherent and finitely generated as an \(\mathcal O_X\)-module.

(c) Let \(f:W\to X\) be an affine morphism. Show that \(f_*\mathcal O_W\) is a quasi-coherent \(\mathcal O_X\)-algebra and that \(W\simeq\Spec f_*\mathcal O_W\) as \(X\)-schemes.

Solution

Here “affine morphism” means: preimages of affine opens are affine. \(\pi_*\mathcal O_Z=\mathcal A\) is understood as an isomorphism of sheaves of \(\mathcal O_X\)-algebras (the \(\mathcal O_X\)-algebra structure of \(\pi_*\mathcal O_Z\) being given by \(\pi^\#\)), and uniqueness is up to unique isomorphism of \(X\)-schemes.

A preliminary fact. Let \(\mathcal A\) be quasi-coherent and \(V\subseteq U\) affine opens of \(X\), \(R=\mathcal O_X(U)\), \(R'=\mathcal O_X(V)\). Then \(\mathcal A(V)=\mathcal A(U)\otimes_RR'\) (via the restriction map). Indeed, apply Prop. 1.14(b) to the open immersion \(j:V\to U\) and the quasi-coherent sheaf \(\mathcal G=\mathcal A|_U=\mathcal A(U)^\sim\): it gives \(j^*\mathcal G=(\mathcal A(U)\otimes_RR')^\sim\), and \(j^*\mathcal G=\mathcal G|_V\) for an open immersion; taking sections over \(V\) gives the claim.

(a) Uniqueness. If \(\pi:Z\to X\) is affine with \(\pi_*\mathcal O_Z=\mathcal A\), then for every affine open \(U\subseteq X\), \(\pi^{-1}(U)\) is an affine scheme with ring \(\mathcal O_Z(\pi^{-1}U)=\mathcal A(U)\), and \(\pi|:\pi^{-1}U\to U\) corresponds to the structure map \(\mathcal O_X(U)\to\mathcal A(U)\). Hence \(\pi^{-1}(U)\simeq\Spec\mathcal A(U)\) as \(U\)-schemes, canonically, and these isomorphisms are compatible with the inclusions \(\pi^{-1}(V)\subseteq\pi^{-1}(U)\) (for \(V\subseteq U\)) because both correspond to the restriction map \(\mathcal A(U)\to\mathcal A(V)\). A scheme is determined up to unique isomorphism by an open cover and the gluing data, so \(Z\) is unique.

Existence. For every affine open \(U\subseteq X\) put \(Z_U=\Spec\mathcal A(U)\) with its structure morphism \(\pi_U:Z_U\to U\) given by \(\mathcal O_X(U)\to\mathcal A(U)\). If \(V\subseteq U\) are affine, the preliminary fact says \(\mathcal A(V)=\mathcal A(U)\otimes_{\mathcal O_X(U)}\mathcal O_X(V)\), hence \(Z_V=Z_U\times_UV=\pi_U^{-1}(V)\), an open subscheme of \(Z_U\); call \(\iota_{VU}:Z_V\to Z_U\) this open immersion over \(X\). By construction \(\iota_{UU}=\operatorname{id}\) and \(\iota_{VU}\iota_{V'V}=\iota_{V'U}\) for \(V'\subseteq V\subseteq U\) (all maps are induced by restriction maps of \(\mathcal A\), which are transitive). For two affine opens \(U,U'\), the open subscheme \(\pi_U^{-1}(U\cap U')\subseteq Z_U\) is the union of the images of the \(Z_V\), \(V\subseteq U\cap U'\) affine, and similarly for \(U'\); the isomorphisms \(\iota_{VU}(Z_V)\simeq Z_V\simeq\iota_{VU'}(Z_V)\) agree on \(Z_{V''}\) for \(V''\subseteq V\) affine, hence glue (two affines \(V_1,V_2\subseteq U\cap U'\) have \(V_1\cap V_2\) covered by affines \(V''\subseteq V_1\cap V_2\) on which both identifications are \(\iota_{V''U}\iota_{V''U'}^{-1}\)) to isomorphisms \(\varphi_{UU'}:\pi_U^{-1}(U\cap U')\to\pi_{U'}^{-1}(U\cap U')\) satisfying the cocycle condition on triple overlaps (check on the \(Z_V\) with \(V\) affine inside \(U\cap U'\cap U''\), where it is the transitivity above). Glue the \(Z_U\) along the \(\varphi_{UU'}\) to a scheme \(Z\) with a morphism \(\pi:Z\to X\) (the \(\pi_U\) agree on overlaps). Then \(\pi^{-1}(U)=Z_U\) is affine for every affine open \(U\) of \(X\), so \(\pi\) is affine, and \((\pi_*\mathcal O_Z)(U)=\mathcal O(Z_U)=\mathcal A(U)\) compatibly with restrictions and with the \(\mathcal O_X\)-algebra structures. Since the affine opens form a base of the topology, \(\pi_*\mathcal O_Z\simeq\mathcal A\).

(b) Let \(\pi:Z=\Spec\mathcal A\to X\). Recall that \(\pi\) is finite if for every affine open \(U\subseteq X\), \(\pi^{-1}(U)\) is affine and \(\mathcal O_Z(\pi^{-1}U)\) is a finite \(\mathcal O_X(U)\)-module (Ex. 3.3.15 shows that it is equivalent to ask this for the members of one affine open cover of \(X\); this is also visible from the argument below).

(\(\Leftarrow\)) Suppose \(\mathcal A\) is quasi-coherent and finitely generated. By Prop. 1.11, \(\mathcal A(U)\) is finitely generated over \(\mathcal O_X(U)\) for every affine open \(U\) ((ii)\(\Rightarrow\)(iii)). Since \(\pi^{-1}(U)=\Spec\mathcal A(U)\) by (a), \(\pi\) is finite.

(\(\Rightarrow\)) \(\mathcal A=\pi_*\mathcal O_Z\) is quasi-coherent by hypothesis in the definition of \(\Spec\mathcal A\). If \(\pi\) is finite, there is an affine open cover \(\{U_i\}\) of \(X\) with \(\mathcal A(U_i)\) finitely generated over \(\mathcal O_X(U_i)\), say by \(n_i\) elements. Then \(\mathcal O_X^{n_i}|_{U_i}\to\mathcal A|_{U_i}=\mathcal A(U_i)^\sim\) is surjective (Prop. 1.5(b)), so \(\mathcal A\) is finitely generated (Def. 1.10).

(c) \(f\) is affine, hence quasi-compact; and it is separated since for affine open \(V\subseteq X\), \(f^{-1}(V)=\Spec B\) with \(B\) an \(\mathcal O_X(V)\)-algebra and the diagonal of \(\Spec B\to\Spec\mathcal O_X(V)\) corresponds to the surjection \(B\otimes B\to B\), a closed immersion. \(\mathcal O_W\) is quasi-coherent on \(W\), so \(f_*\mathcal O_W\) is quasi-coherent on \(X\) by Prop. 1.14(c). It is a sheaf of commutative \(\mathcal O_X\)-algebras via \(f^\#\). By (a), \(Z=\Spec f_*\mathcal O_W\to X\) exists. For each affine open \(U\subseteq X\), \(f^{-1}(U)\) is an affine scheme, so the canonical morphism \(\psi_U:f^{-1}(U)\to\Spec\mathcal O_W(f^{-1}U)=\Spec(f_*\mathcal O_W)(U)=Z_U=\pi^{-1}(U)\) is an isomorphism of \(U\)-schemes. For \(V\subseteq U\) affine, the maps \(\psi_U,\psi_V\) are compatible with the inclusions \(f^{-1}V\subseteq f^{-1}U\) and \(\pi^{-1}V\subseteq\pi^{-1}U\) (functoriality of \(T\mapsto\Spec\mathcal O(T)\) for the open immersion \(f^{-1}V\to f^{-1}U\) of affine schemes). Hence the \(\psi_U\) agree on overlaps, and glue to an isomorphism \(W\to Z\) of \(X\)-schemes. ∎

Exercise 1.18

Problem

Let \(X\) be a Noetherian or separated and quasi-compact scheme. Let \(\mathcal L\) be an invertible sheaf on \(X\) and fix a section \(s\in\mathcal L(X)\).

(a) For \(n\ge1\) write \(\mathcal L^n=\mathcal L^{\otimes n}\) and \(\mathcal L^{\otimes0}=\mathcal O_X\). Let \(\mathcal A=\bigoplus_{n\ge0}\mathcal L^{\otimes n}\). Show that \(\mathcal A\) has a natural structure of \(\mathcal O_X\)-algebra. Let \(Z=\Spec\mathcal A\) and \(\pi:Z\to X\) the canonical morphism.
