    Let \(Z\to Y\) be a faithfully flat morphism. Show that \(f\) is cohomologically flat if and only if \(X\times_YZ\to Z\) is cohomologically flat.

  3. (c)

    Let us suppose that \(Y\) is local with closed point \(y\), and that \(H^0(X_y,\mathcal O_{X_y})\) is a direct sum of fields isomorphic to \(k(y)\). Show that

    \[ H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\otimes_{\mathcal O_{Y,y}}k(y)\to H^0(X_y,\mathcal O_{X_y})\tag{3.17} \]

    is surjective (use Lemma 3.14 with \(A=H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\)).

  4. (d)

    Let \(Y\) be local with closed point \(y\). Let \(R\) be a finite etale algebra over \(k(y)\). Show that there exists a finite free (hence faithfully flat) \(\mathcal O_{Y,y}\)-algebra \(A\) such that \(A\otimes_{\mathcal O_{Y,y}}k(y)=R\). Deduce from this that if \(X_y\) is geometrically reduced, then (3.17) is surjective (consider \(R=H^0(X_y,\mathcal O_{X_y})\) and \(X_A\to\operatorname{Spec}A\)).

  5. (e)

    Let us suppose \(Y\) is regular of dimension \(1\) and that either \(X_y\) is geometrically reduced for every closed point \(y\in Y\), or \(X\) is a complete intersection in some \(\mathbf P^d_Y\). Show that \(f\) is cohomologically flat.

Solution

Reformulation. Cohomological flatness of \(f\) means that for every morphism \(\pi:T\to Y\) the canonical map \(\pi^*f_*\mathcal O_X\to f_{T*}\mathcal O_{X_T}\) is an isomorphism. This is local on \(T\), so (taking \(T\) affine and mapping into an affine open) it is equivalent to:

(CF) for every affine open \(V=\operatorname{Spec}A\subseteq Y\), with \(B=H^0(f^{-1}V,\mathcal O_X)\), and every \(A\)-algebra \(A'\), the canonical map \(\varphi_{A'}:B\otimes_AA'\to H^0(X_{A'},\mathcal O_{X_{A'}})\) is bijective (\(X_{A'}=X_V\times_V\operatorname{Spec}A'\)).

(a) If \(f\) is cohomologically flat and \(y\in Y\), put \(Y_y=\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal O_{Y,y}\), \(X^y=X\times_YY_y\). For an affine open \(V=\operatorname{Spec}A\ni y\), \(H^0(X^y,\mathcal O)=B\otimes_AA_{\mathfrak p}\) (Cor. 2.27), and for an \(A_{\mathfrak p}\)-algebra \(A'\) the map \(\varphi_{A'}\) for \(f^y\) is \(B\otimes_AA_{\mathfrak p}\otimes_{A_{\mathfrak p}}A'=B\otimes_AA'\to H^0(X_{A'},\mathcal O)\), i.e. the one for \(f\) with \(A'\) regarded as an \(A\)-algebra. So \(f^y\) satisfies (CF). Conversely, let \(V=\operatorname{Spec}A\subseteq Y\) be affine and \(A'\) an \(A\)-algebra. The map \(\varphi_{A'}\) is a homomorphism of \(A\)-modules, bijective iff \(\varphi_{A'}\otimes_AA_{\mathfrak p}\) is bijective for all primes \(\mathfrak p\). By flat base change (Cor. 2.27) \(\varphi_{A'}\otimes_AA_{\mathfrak p}\) is the map \(\varphi_{A'_{\mathfrak p}}\) of \(f^y\) (\(y\leftrightarrow\mathfrak p\)) for the \(A_{\mathfrak p}\)-algebra \(A'_{\mathfrak p}=A'\otimes_AA_{\mathfrak p}\), which is bijective by hypothesis.

(b) Let \(\rho:Z\to Y\) be faithfully flat and \(f_Z:X_Z\to Z\). Let \(W=\operatorname{Spec}C\subseteq Z\) be an affine open mapping into an affine open \(V=\operatorname{Spec}A\) of \(Y\); \(C\) is flat over \(A\), \(H^0(X_W,\mathcal O)=B\otimes_AC\) (Cor. 2.27), and for a \(C\)-algebra \(C'\) the map \(\varphi^Z_{C'}:(B\otimes_AC)\otimes_CC'=B\otimes_AC'\to H^0(X_{C'},\mathcal O)\) is \(\varphi_{C'}\), with \(C'\) regarded as an \(A\)-algebra. (\(\Rightarrow\)) If \(f\) satisfies (CF), then so does \(f_Z\) (for all such \(W\), hence on \(Z\)). (\(\Leftarrow\)) Let \(V=\operatorname{Spec}A\subseteq Y\) be affine, \(A'\) an \(A\)-algebra, and \(\mathfrak p\) a prime of \(A\). Let \(z\in Z\) lie over \(\mathfrak p\) (\(\rho\) is surjective), \(W=\operatorname{Spec}C\ni z\) an affine open mapping into \(V\), and \(C'=A'\otimes_AC\). Since \(A'\to C'\) is flat, \(H^0(X_{C'},\mathcal O)=H^0(X_{A'},\mathcal O)\otimes_{A'}C'=H^0(X_{A'},\mathcal O)\otimes_AC\) (Cor. 2.27), and \(\varphi^Z_{C'}=\varphi_{A'}\otimes_AC\). By (CF) for \(f_Z\) this is bijective. Localizing at \(z\): \(\varphi_{A'}\otimes_A\mathcal O_{Z,z}\) is bijective. The local homomorphism \(A_{\mathfrak p}\to\mathcal O_{Z,z}\) is flat, hence faithfully flat, so \(\varphi_{A'}\otimes_AA_{\mathfrak p}\) is bijective (its kernel and cokernel become \(0\) after the faithfully flat extension, so are \(0\)). As \(\mathfrak p\) was arbitrary, \(\varphi_{A'}\) is bijective.

(c) Let \(Y=\operatorname{Spec}A\) with \(A\) local Noetherian, \(\mathfrak m\) its maximal ideal, \(k=k(y)=A/\mathfrak m\), \(B=H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\). By Step 0 of Ex. 3.10, \(B=\mathcal A(Y)\) is a finite \(A\)-algebra, \(Y'=\operatorname{Spec}B\), and \(g:X\to\operatorname{Spec}B\) is projective with \(g_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_{\operatorname{Spec}B}\); so \(B\) is a Noetherian ring, \(X\) is projective over \(B\), and \(B\to H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\) is the identity, in particular purely inseparable. Apply Lemma 3.14 to the ring \(B\) and the ideal \(I=\mathfrak mB\): there is an ideal \(J\) of \(B\) with \(J\subseteq\mathfrak mB\subseteq\sqrt J\) such that \(B/J\to R_J:=H^0(X,\mathcal O_X/J\mathcal O_X)\) is purely inseparable. Let \(X_J\) be the closed subscheme of \(X\) defined by \(J\mathcal O_X\). As \(\sqrt J=\sqrt{\mathfrak mB}\), \(X_J\) and \(X_y=X\otimes_Ak\) (defined by \(\mathfrak m\mathcal O_X=\mathfrak mB\,\mathcal O_X\)) have the same underlying space, and \(X_y\subseteq X_J\) is a closed subscheme. Also \(B/J\) is Artinian (\(J\supseteq(\mathfrak mB)^n\) for some \(n\) and \(B/\mathfrak mB\) is a finite \(k\)-algebra), and \(R_J\) is a finite \(B/J\)-module (Thm. 3.2), hence Artinian.

Components. The space \(|X_y|\) is Noetherian, with finitely many connected components \(X_1,\dots,X_N\), all open and closed. So \(H^0(X_y,\mathcal O)=\prod_iH^0(X_i,\mathcal O)\), each factor non-zero without idempotents other than \(0,1\) (Ex. 2.4.6). By hypothesis this ring is \(\simeq k^m\), a product of \(m\) fields \(k\); a decomposition of \(k^m\) into \(N\) non-zero rings without non-trivial idempotents has \(N=m\) factors, each equal to \(k\). So \(H^0(X_i,\mathcal O)=k\) for all \(i\). The same components \(X_i^J\) are open and closed in \(X_J\), so \(R_J=\prod_iR_i\) with \(R_i=H^0(X_i^J,\mathcal O)\), Artinian without non-trivial idempotents, hence local, with residue field \(\kappa_i\).

Residue fields. The restriction to the closed subscheme \(X_i\subseteq X_i^J\) gives a ring homomorphism \(R_i\to H^0(X_i,\mathcal O)=k\) over \(A\); it factors through a homomorphism \(\sigma_i:\kappa_i\to k\) (its kernel is the maximal ideal). The elements of \(\mathfrak m\) are nilpotent in \(R_i\) (\(\mathfrak m\subseteq\sqrt J\) and \(JR_i=0\)), so \(A\to R_i\to\kappa_i\) factors through \(k\): we get \(\tau_i:k\to\kappa_i\), and \(\sigma_i\tau_i=\operatorname{id}_k\) (both are \(A\)-algebra maps from \(k=A/\mathfrak m\)). A field homomorphism \(\sigma_i\) with a section is bijective, so \(\kappa_i\simeq k\) via \(\sigma_i\), with inverse \(\tau_i\).

Points of \(\operatorname{Spec}B\) over \(y\). Since \(B/J\to R_J\) is purely inseparable, \(\operatorname{Spec}R_J=\bigsqcup\operatorname{Spec}R_i\to\operatorname{Spec}B/J\) is injective, so its \(N\) points map to \(N\) distinct points \(z_1,\dots,z_N\) of \(\operatorname{Spec}B/J\), with \(\kappa_i/k(z_i)\) purely inseparable. The composition \(k\to k(z_i)\to\kappa_i\simeq k\) is the identity, so \(k(z_i)=k\). Every point \(z\) of \(\operatorname{Spec}B/J=h^{-1}(y)\) is of the form \(g(x)\) with \(x\in X\) (\(g\) is surjective), and \(x\in X_y=|X_J|\), say \(x\in X_i\); the morphism \(g|_{X_J}\) factors as \(X_J\to\operatorname{Spec}R_J\to\operatorname{Spec}B/J\), so \(z=z_i\). Thus \(\operatorname{Spec}B/J=\{z_1,\dots,z_N\}\), and \(B/\mathfrak mB\) is an Artinian ring with exactly \(N\) maximal ideals, all with residue field \(k\). By the Chinese remainder theorem \(B/\mathfrak mB\to\prod_ik(z_i)=k^N\) is surjective.

Conclusion. The map \(B\to H^0(X_y,\mathcal O)=\prod_iH^0(X_i,\mathcal O)=k^N\) (restriction of sections) factors through \(B/\mathfrak mB\) and, in the \(i\)-th component, is \(B\to R_i\to\kappa_i\xrightarrow{\sigma_i}k\), which is \(B\to k(z_i)=k\). So it is the surjection above, and (3.17) is surjective.

(d) Write \(R=\prod_{j=1}^nk_j\) with \(k_j\) finite separable field extensions of \(k=k(y)\), \(k_j=k[T]/(\tilde P_j)\) with \(\tilde P_j\) monic irreducible (primitive element theorem). Let \(P_j\in\mathcal O_{Y,y}[T]\) be monic lifts of \(\tilde P_j\) and \(A=\prod_j\mathcal O_{Y,y}[T]/(P_j)\). It is a free \(\mathcal O_{Y,y}\)-module of rank \(\sum\deg P_j\), hence faithfully flat, and \(A\otimes k=\prod_jk[T]/(\tilde P_j)=R\).

Now let \(X_y\) be geometrically reduced and \(R_0=H^0(X_y,\mathcal O_{X_y})\), a finite \(k\)-algebra (Thm. 3.2). With \(\kappa=\bar k\), \(R_0\otimes_k\kappa=H^0(X_\kappa,\mathcal O)\) (Cor. 2.27), a reduced ring because \(X_\kappa\) is reduced. So \(R_0\) is reduced and Artinian, \(R_0=\prod_jk_j\) with fields \(k_j\), and each \(k_j\otimes_k\kappa\) is reduced, so \(k_j/k\) is separable: \(R_0\) is finite etale. Choose a finite Galois extension \(E/k\) containing a copy of every \(k_j\); then \(k_j\otimes_kE\simeq E^{[k_j:k]}\), hence \(R_0\otimes_kE\simeq E^{\dim_kR_0}\). Apply the construction above to the single field \(E=k[T]/(\tilde P)\): \(A_0=\mathcal O_{Y,y}[T]/(P)\) is finite free over \(\mathcal O_{Y,y}\) with \(A_0\otimes k=E\); it is a local ring (it is finite over a local ring and \(A_0/\mathfrak mA_0=E\) is a field), with closed point \(y_0\) and \(k(y_0)=E\). Let \(X_0=X\times_YY_0\), \(Y_0=\operatorname{Spec}A_0\). It is projective over the local Noetherian scheme \(Y_0\) and its closed fibre is \(X_y\otimes_kE\) with \(H^0=R_0\otimes_kE\simeq E^{\dim R_0}\), a direct sum of fields isomorphic to \(k(y_0)\). By (c), \(H^0(X_0,\mathcal O)\otimes_{A_0}E\to H^0(X_y\otimes E,\mathcal O)\) is surjective. By Cor. 2.27 (\(A_0\) is flat over \(\mathcal O_{Y,y}\)), \(H^0(X_0,\mathcal O)=B\otimes A_0\), so this map is \(\bigl(B\otimes_{\mathcal O_{Y,y}}k\to H^0(X_y,\mathcal O)\bigr)\otimes_kE\), i.e. (3.17) tensored with \(E\). As \(E\) is faithfully flat over \(k\), (3.17) is surjective.

(e) Reduction. By (a) it suffices to treat \(Y_y=\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal O_{Y,y}\) for each \(y\) (\(X^y\to Y_y\) is again flat, projective, and satisfies the hypothesis). As \(Y\) is regular of dimension \(1\), \(\mathcal O_{Y,y}\) is a discrete valuation ring (closed point) or a field (generic point). If \(\mathcal O_{Y,y}\) is a field \(K\), every \(K\)-algebra \(A'\) is flat over \(K\), so \(\varphi_{A'}\) is bijective by Lemma 2.26, Cor. 2.27; (CF) holds. So let \(S=\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal O_K\), \(\mathcal O_K=\mathcal O_{Y,y}\) a discrete valuation ring with closed point \(s\), \(f:X\to S\) flat projective. The sheaf \(\mathcal O_X\) is coherent and flat over \(S\). For an \(\mathcal O_K\)-module \(M\), \(H^0(X,\mathcal O_X\otimes M)\) is \(H^0(X_{M},\mathcal O)\) if \(M\) is an algebra (\(X_M\to X\) affine, Ex. 2.3), so (CF) for \(f\) says exactly that the map \(\rho_M\) of Thm. 3.20(c) is bijective for \(p=0\), \(\mathcal F=\mathcal O_X\), and all algebras \(M=A'\). By Thm. 3.20(c) (and Cor. 3.22), it is enough to show that

\[ H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\otimes k(s)\to H^0(X_s,\mathcal O_{X_s})\tag{$*$} \]

is surjective ((3.14) for \(p=0\)).

First case: \(X_s\) is geometrically reduced. Then \((*)\) is surjective by (d) (applied to the local scheme \(S\)).

Second case: \(X=V_+(F_1,\dots,F_c)\subseteq\mathbf P^d_S\) is a complete intersection, which we understand (as in Ex. 3.4 and 3.5) as: every fibre \(X_y\) has dimension \(r=d-c\). If \(r=0\), then \(f\) is proper with finite fibres, hence finite, hence affine; then \(H^0(X_{A'},\mathcal O)=B\otimes_AA'\) trivially and (CF) holds. If \(r\ge1\), then by Ex. 3.3(b)(2) (and the argument of Ex. 3.3(c)) \(H^0(X_s,\mathcal O_{X_s})=k(s)\), spanned by the image of \(1\), and \((*)\) is surjective because \(1\in H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\) maps to \(1\).

In both cases \(f\) is cohomologically flat. ∎

Exercise 3.15

Problem

(A non-cohomologically flat scheme). Let \(S=\operatorname{Spec}A\) be a Noetherian integral affine scheme, and let \(t\in A\) be a non-zero non-invertible element. Let us consider the \(S\)-scheme

\[ X=\operatorname{Proj}A[w,x,y,z]/(x^2-t^2wy,\ xz-ty^2,\ xy-twz,\ z^2w-y^3), \]

and let \(f:X\to S\) denote the structural morphism.

  1. (a)

    Show that over the open subset \(T:=D(t)\) of \(S\), there exists a morphism \(\mathbf P^1_T\to\mathbf P^3_T\) that induces an isomorphism \(\mathbf P^1_T\simeq X\times_ST\) (consider the \(3\)-uple embedding \(\mathbf P^1_T\to\mathbf P^3_T\) (Ex. 1.27) followed by the automorphism \((w,x,y,z)\to(w,tx,y,z)\)).

  2. (b)

    Show that \(D_+(z)\cap X\simeq\mathbf A^1_S\) and that \(\mathcal O_X(D_+(w)\cap X)\) is free over \(A\) as an \(A\)-module. Conclude that \(X\) is flat over \(S\).

  3. (c)

    Let \(s\in V(t)\). Show that \(X_s\) is an irreducible curve, reduced except at the point \((1,0,0,0)\). Show that we have \(\dim_{k(s)}H^0(X_s,\mathcal O_{X_s})=2\) and that \(A\to H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\) is an isomorphism. Deduce from this that \(X\to S\) is not cohomologically flat.

  4. (d)

    Show that \(X\) is not normal, and that its normalization is isomorphic to \(\mathbf P^1_S\) if \(S\) is normal (Ex. 4.1.5).

Solution

Let \(I\) be the homogeneous ideal generated by the four relations, \(B_0=A[w,x,y,z]/I\) so \(X=\operatorname{Proj}B_0\). Write \(A[u,v]\) for the homogeneous coordinate ring of \(\mathbf P^1_A\) and \(s=v/u\) for the affine coordinate on \(D_+(u)\). Define

\[ \nu:\mathbf P^1_S\to\mathbf P^3_S,\qquad(u:v)\mapsto(w:x:y:z)=(u^3:t\,u^2v:uv^2:v^3). \]

The four sections \(u^3,tu^2v,uv^2,v^3\) of \(\mathcal O(3)\) have no common zero (already \(u^3,v^3\) have none), so \(\nu\) is a morphism. The four relations of \(I\) vanish when we substitute these four elements:

\begin{align*} x^2-t^2wy&\mapsto t^2u^4v^2-t^2u^3\cdot uv^2=0, & xz-ty^2&\mapsto tu^2v\cdot v^3-t\,u^2v^4=0,\\ xy-twz&\mapsto t\,u^2v\cdot uv^2-t\,u^3v^3=0, & z^2w-y^3&\mapsto v^6u^3-u^3v^6=0. \end{align*}

Hence \(\nu\) factors through \(X\): \(\nu:\mathbf P^1_S\to X\).

Charts of \(X\). The sets \(D_+(w)\cap X\) and \(D_+(z)\cap X\) cover \(X\): if a homogeneous prime \(\mathfrak P\) of \(B_0\) contains \(w\) and \(z\), then it contains \(x^2=t^2wy+(x^2-t^2wy)\) and \(y^3=z^2w-(z^2w-y^3)\), hence \(x,y\), hence the whole irrelevant ideal; so it is not a point of \(X\).

\(D_+(z)\). Setting \(z=1\), the relations become \(x^2-t^2wy\), \(x-ty^2\), \(xy-tw\), \(w-y^3\). The last two eliminate \(w=y^3\) and \(x=ty^2\), and the other two relations then read \(t^2y^4-t^2y^4=0\) and \(ty^3-ty^3=0\). Hence the coordinate ring of \(D_+(z)\cap X\) is \(A[y]\), and \(D_+(z)\cap X\simeq\mathbf A^1_S\); \(\nu^{-1}(D_+(z))=D_+(v)=\operatorname{Spec}A[Y]\), \(Y=u/v=s^{-1}\), and \(\nu\) restricts to an isomorphism \(D_+(v)\to D_+(z)\cap X\) (\(y/z=Y\)).

\(D_+(w)\). Setting \(w=1\) we get \(C:=A[x,y,z]/(x^2-t^2y,\ xz-ty^2,\ xy-tz,\ z^2-y^3)\). The \(A\)-algebra map \(\theta:C\to A[s]\), \(x\mapsto ts\), \(y\mapsto s^2\), \(z\mapsto s^3\), is well defined (the four relations are satisfied: \(t^2s^2=t^2s^2\), \(ts\cdot s^3=t\,s^4\), \(ts\cdot s^2=t\,s^3\), \(s^6=s^6\)); it is the restriction of \(\nu\) to \(\nu^{-1}(D_+(w))=D_+(u)=\operatorname{Spec}A[s]\). We claim that \(C\) is spanned over \(A\) by \(\mathcal B=\{1,\ x\}\cup\{y^b\mid b\ge1\}\cup\{y^bz\mid b\ge0\}\). Using \(x^2=t^2y\), \(xy=tz\), \(xz=ty^2\), every monomial \(x^ay^bz^c\) is an \(A\)-combination of monomials whose \(x\)-degree is smaller, unless it is \(x\) or has \(a=0\) already; and for \(a=0\), \(z^2=y^3\) reduces \(c\) to \(0\) or \(1\). Now \(\theta\) sends \(\mathcal B\) to \(1,\ ts,\ s^{2b}\ (b\ge1),\ s^{2b+3}\ (b\ge0)\), i.e. to \(1\), \(ts\) and each \(s^k\), \(k\ge2\), exactly once. These elements are \(A\)-linearly independent in \(A[s]\): a relation \(a_0+a_1\,ts+\sum_{k\ge2}a_ks^k=0\) in \(A[s]\) gives \(a_0=0\), \(a_1t=0\) and \(a_k=0\) for \(k\ge2\), and then \(a_1=0\) because \(A\) is a domain and \(t\ne0\). Hence \(\mathcal B\) is an \(A\)-basis of \(C\), \(\theta\) is injective, and

\[ C\simeq A[ts,s^2,s^3]\subseteq A[s],\qquad C\text{ is a free }A\text{-module with basis }\mathcal B. \]

(b) We have shown \(D_+(z)\cap X\simeq\mathbf A^1_S\) and that \(\mathcal O_X(D_+(w)\cap X)=C\) (the coordinate ring of this affine open) is free over \(A\) with basis \(\mathcal B\). Both affine opens have flat, even free, coordinate rings over \(A\), and they cover \(X\), so \(X\) is flat over \(S\).

(a) Over \(T=D(t)\), \(t\) is invertible. Then \(C\otimes_AA_t=A_t[ts,s^2,s^3]=A_t[s]\) (as \(s=t^{-1}\cdot ts\)) so \(\theta\) becomes an isomorphism \(C_t\to A_t[s]\), i.e. \(\nu:D_+(u)_T\to(D_+(w)\cap X)_T\) is an isomorphism; and \(\nu:D_+(v)_T\to(D_+(z)\cap X)_T\) is an isomorphism (already over \(S\)). Since \(\nu^{-1}(D_+(w))=D_+(u)\), \(\nu^{-1}(D_+(z))=D_+(v)\), and these two cover \(\mathbf P^1\) and \(X\) respectively, \(\nu_T:\mathbf P^1_T\to X_T=X\times_ST\) is an isomorphism. Finally \(\nu\) is the composition of the \(3\)-uple embedding \((u:v)\mapsto(u^3:u^2v:uv^2:v^3)\) with the automorphism \((w,x,y,z)\mapsto(w,tx,y,z)\) of \(\mathbf P^3_T\) (\(t\) is invertible on \(T\)), followed by the inclusion \(X_T\subseteq\mathbf P^3_T\). This is the morphism asked for.

(c) Let \(s\in V(t)\) (non-empty as \(t\) is not invertible), \(k=k(s)\); the image of \(t\) in \(k\) is \(0\). Then \(X_s=\operatorname{Proj}k[w,x,y,z]/(x^2,xz,xy,z^2w-y^3)\), covered by

\(U_1=D_+(z)\cap X_s=\operatorname{Spec}k[y]\) and \(U_2=D_+(w)\cap X_s=\operatorname{Spec}C_s\), \(C_s=C\otimes_Ak=k[x,y,z]/(x^2,xy,xz,z^2-y^3)\).

By the basis \(\mathcal B\) of \(C\), \(C_s\) has \(k\)-basis \(\mathcal B\); we have \(x^2=xy=xz=0\), so \(kx\) is an ideal and \(C_s/kx=\Gamma:=k[y,z]/(z^2-y^3)\simeq k[\sigma^2,\sigma^3]\) (\(y\mapsto\sigma^2\), \(z\mapsto\sigma^3\), \(\sigma\) an indeterminate), a domain. Thus \(C_s=\Gamma\oplus kx\) as a vector space, and the underlying space of \(U_2\) is \(\operatorname{Spec}\Gamma\), irreducible of dimension \(1\). So \(U_1\) and \(U_2\) are irreducible curves with non-empty intersection \(D(z)\) (a non-empty open subset of each), hence \(X_s\) is an irreducible curve.

Reducedness. \(U_1\) is reduced. In \(C_s\), the element \(x\) is nilpotent and non-zero, and the prime ideal \(\mathfrak o=(x,y,z)\) (the point \((1,0,0,0)\)) is the only prime of \(C_s\) containing \(y\) and \(z\) (\(\Gamma/(y,z)=k\)). At any other point, \(y\) or \(z\) is invertible in the local ring, and then \(x=0\) (from \(xy=0\), \(xz=0\)); the local ring is a localization of \(\Gamma\), reduced. At the point \(\mathfrak o\) the local ring has the non-zero nilpotent \(x\) and is not reduced. So \(X_s\) is reduced except at \((1,0,0,0)\).

\(H^0(X_s,\mathcal O)\). \(U_1\cap U_2=D_+(wz)=\operatorname{Spec}(C_s)_z\); in \((C_s)_z\) we have \(x=0\) (as \(xz=0\)) and \(y\) is a unit (as \(y^3=z^2\)), so \((C_s)_z=\Gamma_z=k[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]\). The restriction maps are: \(k[Y]\to k[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]\), \(Y\mapsto y/z=\sigma^{-1}\) (on \(U_1\), \(y/z=Y\) is the coordinate), and \(C_s\to k[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]\), \(x\mapsto0\), \(y\mapsto\sigma^2\), \(z\mapsto\sigma^3\). A global section is a pair \((f(Y),\,p(y,z)+cx)\) (\(f\in k[Y]\), \(p\in\Gamma\), \(c\in k\)) with \(f(\sigma^{-1})=p(\sigma^2,\sigma^3)\) in \(k[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]\). The left side lies in \(k[\sigma^{-1}]\), the right side in \(k[\sigma]\), and \(k[\sigma^{-1}]\cap k[\sigma]=k\); so \(f=\lambda\) is constant, and \(p=\lambda\) because \(\Gamma\to k[\sigma]\) is injective. Thus the global sections are exactly \(\lambda\cdot1+c\cdot\xi\) with \(\xi=(0,x)\), so \(\dim_kH^0(X_s,\mathcal O_{X_s})=2\).

\(H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)=A\). Similarly, \(U_1^A=D_+(z)\cap X=\operatorname{Spec}A[Y]\), \(U_2^A=\operatorname{Spec}C\) and \(U_1^A\cap U_2^A=\operatorname{Spec}C_z\). Now \(C\subseteq A[s]\) and \(C_z=A[ts,s^2,s^3,s^{-3}]\subseteq A[s,s^{-1}]\); it contains \(s^{-1}=y\,z^{-1}\) and \(s=y^2z^{-1}\) (as \(y=s^2\), \(z=s^3\)), thus \(C_z=A[s,s^{-1}]\). All rings are domains inside \(A[s,s^{-1}]\) (\(A[Y]=A[s^{-1}]\) because \(Y\mapsto y/z=s^{-1}\)), so the restriction maps are injective and a global section is an element of \(A[s^{-1}]\cap C\). This lies in \(A[s^{-1}]\cap A[s]=A\), and \(A\subseteq C\). So \(H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)=A\) and \(A\to H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\) is an isomorphism.

Not cohomologically flat. If \(f\) were cohomologically flat, then by (CF) of Ex. 3.14 (the base change \(\operatorname{Spec}k(s)\to S\)) the map \(H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)\otimes_Ak(s)\to H^0(X_s,\mathcal O_{X_s})\) would be bijective. Its source is \(A\otimes_Ak(s)=k(s)\), of dimension \(1\), and its target has dimension \(2\). So \(f\) is not cohomologically flat (although \(f\) is flat and projective by (b)).

(d) The scheme \(X\) is integral: it is covered by the integral opens \(D_+(z)\cap X\simeq\mathbf A^1_S\) (coordinate ring \(A[y]\)) and \(D_+(w)\cap X=\operatorname{Spec}C\) (\(C\subseteq A[s]\)), whose intersection is non-empty (it contains \(X_T\)). Its function field is \(K(X)=\operatorname{Frac}(A)(s)\).

Not normal. The element \(s=z/y\in\operatorname{Frac}C\) satisfies \(s^2=y\in C\), so it is integral over \(C\). But \(s\notin C\): an element of \(C\), written in the basis \(\mathcal B\), has coefficient of \(s\) (in \(A[s]\)) in \(tA\), and \(1\notin tA\) since \(t\) is not invertible. So \(C\) is not integrally closed in its fraction field, hence some localization of \(C\) at a maximal ideal is not integrally closed, and \(X\) is not normal.

Normalization. Assume \(S\) normal, i.e. \(A\) is a normal domain. Then \(\mathbf P^1_S\) is normal (it is covered by two copies of \(\operatorname{Spec}A[s]\), and a polynomial ring over a normal domain is normal) and integral, with function field \(\operatorname{Frac}(A)(s)=K(X)\). The morphism \(\nu:\mathbf P^1_S\to X\) is birational (isomorphism over the dense open \(X_T\) by (a)) and finite: over \(D_+(v)\) it is an isomorphism, and over \(D_+(w)\cap X=\operatorname{Spec}C\) it is \(C\subseteq A[s]\), where \(A[s]=C+Cs\) is a finite \(C\)-module: \(A[s]=C+Cs\), because \(A\) and the \(As^k\) (\(k\ge2\)) lie in \(C\) and \(As\subseteq Cs\). So \(\nu_*\mathcal O_{\mathbf P^1_S}\) is a finite \(\mathcal O_X\)-algebra, contained in \(K(X)\) and integrally closed in it (it is the sheaf of rings of a normal scheme with function field \(K(X)\)); hence it equals the integral closure of \(\mathcal O_X\) in \(K(X)\), and \(\nu\) is the normalization of \(X\) (Ex. 4.1.5). Thus the normalization of \(X\) is \(\mathbf P^1_S\). ∎
